| **11** | ✅ `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp`, and the real-rclcpp-reachability fixes it uncovered - found while provisioning a benchmark comparing `rmw_tickle` against `rmw_fastrtps_cpp`/`rmw_cyclonedds_cpp` via [`ros2/buildfarm_perf_tests`](https://github.com/ros2/buildfarm_perf_tests) (`.github/workflows/rmw-perf.yml`, `.github/scripts/README-rmw-perf.md`), the first time this rmw was ever exercised via a real `rclcpp` C++ application rather than its own C-level unit tests or `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_tests`' own `ROSIDL_GET_MSG_TYPE_SUPPORT()`-based dispatch test (which, it turns out, never went through the code path that was actually broken). **Root cause**: `rclcpp::create_publisher<T>()`/`create_subscription<T>()` always start from the C++-level `"rosidl_typesupport_cpp"` dispatch, whose per-message candidate list is fixed at generate time from whichever packages are registered as a `"rosidl_typesupport_cpp"` `ament_index` resource - `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c` (Milestone 1) only ever registered itself as a `"rosidl_typesupport_c"` resource, one level lower, structurally unreachable from that dispatch no matter how correct its own generated code was. Fixed with a new `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp` package: registers `"rosidl_typesupport_cpp"`, and its own per-message generated wrapper (`cmake/rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp_generate_interfaces.cmake` + `resource/msg__type_support.cpp.in`, a `configure_file()` template needing no per-field codegen at all) calls straight into the already-generated `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c` symbol via a real link-time call - *not* a second hop through `rosidl_typesupport_cpp`'s own generic map-walking dispatch, which (confirmed by fetching `ros2/rosidl_typesupport`'s real `type_support_dispatch.hpp` source) only ever fires for a handle whose *own* identifier is the literal string `"rosidl_typesupport_cpp"`, not a generic "any handle can nest one more level" primitive. `rmw_typesupport.c`'s `rmw_tickle_get_message_callbacks()` now tries `"rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp"` first, falling back to the plain `"_c"` identifier for lower-level/C-only callers. New `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_tests/test/test_dispatch_cpp.cpp` closes the blind spot for good: it starts from `rosidl_typesupport_cpp::get_message_type_support_handle<T>()` (the actual entry point a real rclcpp app uses), unlike `test_dispatch.c`'s own `ROSIDL_GET_MSG_TYPE_SUPPORT()` macro (a C-only entry point that was never affected by the gap). Once real rclcpp nodes could actually reach `rmw_tickle` for the first time, several more gaps surfaced, all fixed here too: (a) `rmw_qos.c` - a real `rclcpp::Node` unconditionally creates internal services (e.g. the type description service) at `RELIABLE` with no way to opt out, so `rmw_tickle_validate_qos_profile()` now takes an `rmw_tickle_entity_kind_t` and accepts `RELIABLE` for services/clients specifically (backed by `tt_Client_call()`'s own existing bounded retry - a real, if bounded, delivery-assurance mechanism topics simply don't have), while still rejecting it for publishers/subscriptions; (b) `rmw_publisher_get_actual_qos`/`rmw_subscription_get_actual_qos`, `rmw_get_gid_for_publisher`, and `rmw_publisher_event_init`/`rmw_subscription_event_init` (returning `RMW_RET_UNSUPPORTED` per `rmw/event.h`'s own documented contract) - all called unconditionally by a real `rclcpp::Publisher`/`Subscription` construction, unlike most other optional `rmw_*()` extras `rclcpp` merely probes and tolerates the absence of; (c) `CMakeLists.txt` - `ament_target_dependencies()` has been fully removed on some newer ROS 2 distros (found on the benchmark rig's own OS, Ubuntu 26.04's `lyrical`), switched to modern imported targets matching real `rmw_cyclonedds_cpp`'s own style; (d) `include/tickle/tickle.h` - `_Alignas` (a C11 keyword, not valid C++) replaced with a `tt_ALIGNAS()` macro so the header stays includable from C++ (needed by `test_dispatch_cpp.cpp`). Verified end-to-end: two-process `Array1k`/`Struct16` pub-sub round trips (`buildfarm_perf_tests`' own two-OS-process test shape, sidestepping Milestone 2's "one node per process" limit) complete successfully for `rmw_tickle` alongside `rmw_fastrtps_cpp` and `rmw_cyclonedds_cpp`, both `async`/`sync` modes. **Known, pre-existing gaps this did not need to fix** (surfaced by the same benchmark, out of scope here): single-process `perf_test` mode needs two `rclcpp::Node`s in one process (Milestone 2's own deferred limitation); `test_spinning`'s own launch path doesn't disable `/rosout` the way `performance_test`'s own nodes do (a local, unpushed patch - see `README-rmw-perf.md`); the cross-vendor `test_pub_sub_*_ROS2` test needs the *service*-side counterpart of this same C++-reachability fix (`.srv` was deliberately left out of `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp`'s own first cut - see its own CMake extension's top comment) | 1, 3 | ✅ done |

| **12** | ✅ Two real, previously-hidden bugs found by getting Milestone 11's benchmark to actually produce non-zero numbers - a passing two-process `buildfarm_perf_tests` run had been silently exchanging *zero* messages the whole time (`assert_wait_for_successful_exit()` only checks the exit code, never actual data flow). **(a) Publish throughput starved to near-zero under sustained load.** `poll_thread_main()`'s own `lock() -> tt_Node_poll() -> unlock()` loop (Milestone 2) re-locks the same mutex again immediately with no gap - measured (not guessed): this starves any *other* thread blocked in `pthread_mutex_lock()` on the same mutex (`rmw_publish()` et al.) for tens to hundreds of milliseconds at a time, because glibc's mutex makes no fairness guarantee and a thread re-locking a mutex it just released can win the race against a parked waiter's own futex wake+reschedule far more often than intuition suggests - especially with this call pattern's very short critical section and high (~1000/s) frequency. `sched_yield()` does *not* fix it (Linux's CFS treats it as close to a no-op); a real, if tiny (1us), `nanosleep()` between `unlock()` and the next `lock()` does - restored `perf_test`'s own `-t Array1k` publish rate from single digits/sec back to the full requested ~1000/s. **(b) Two `rmw_tickle` nodes on the same host silently drop 100% of each other's traffic.** `tt_get_node_id()`'s own auto-detect derives a node's id from the last octet of its address on the broadcast subnet (`include/tickle/config.h`'s own, pre-existing doc comment already named this exact failure mode) - correct for two real separate hosts (the actual `tickle-hil` rig), but `buildfarm_perf_tests`' own "two-process" topology runs *both* sides on one host, so both derived the identical id and each treated the other's every packet as "self sent" (`process_datagram()`, `tickle.c`) - a real, silent, zero-throughput failure mode, not a crash. `examples/linux/*`'s own standalone binaries already expose a `-I node_id` CLI flag for exactly this; an `rmw` plugin has no CLI of its own to extend, so added the same override as an environment variable instead - `TICKLE_NODE_ID`, read once in `rmw_init()`, mirroring the existing `TICKLE_BROADCAST_ADDR` pattern. `rmw-perf.yml`'s own benchmark rig now sets a distinct one per side (`README-rmw-perf.md`'s provisioning patch (d)) - not solved by rmw_tickle itself, since there's no general way for two independently-launched processes to agree on distinct ids without something external assigning them. Verified: the same two-process `Array1k`/`Struct16` runs Milestone 11 got passing now report real, sane numbers (received message counts matching sent counts, non-zero latency/throughput) instead of silent zeros, for the first time this benchmark has produced anything meaningful | 2, 11 | ✅ done |
| **13** | ✅ Chased `rmw_tickle`'s own remaining latency gap to `rmw_fastrtps_cpp`/`rmw_cyclonedds_cpp` (Milestone 12 fixed *zero-throughput*, not the ~13x-slower-than-DDS latency itself - Array1k averaged ~0.44ms vs DDS's ~0.03ms). Two TickLE-core changes, both in `DESIGN.md`'s "RPC and Publish flush immediately by default; batching is opt-in": (a) `tt_Publisher_publish()` now flushes every call immediately by default (new `tt_Publisher.batch` field, opt-in escape hatch for a genuinely high-rate small-message Publisher - measured ~4.4x throughput cost of *not* batching there); (b) `tt_receive()` switched `poll()` for `ppoll()` (`hal_linux.c`) so a sub-millisecond wait no longer silently rounds up to a full millisecond. Real methodology trap found and fixed along the way, worth recording: `~/rmw_perf_ws`'s own generated `install/setup.sh` had picked up a stale parent-prefix chain pointing at a *different* checkout's `rmw_tickle` build (from interleaving manual `colcon build`s with `gh run watch`-monitored `rmw-perf.yml` CI dispatches in the same shell earlier) - `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` resolved that stale copy ahead of the freshly-rebuilt one, so several rounds of "no measurable change" were actually re-measuring the *same* unchanged library; adding diagnostic prints to `rmw_wait()`/`rmw_take_with_info()` and finding they never fired is what surfaced it. Re-measured cleanly (fresh library confirmed loaded, both diagnostics firing) after the fix: Array1k two-process latency dropped ~9x (0.44ms → 0.048ms), landing within ~1.5x of both DDS vendors on the same rig, zero message loss, all four Array1k/Struct16 x async/sync combinations consistent within noise | 12 | ✅ done |
| **14** | ✅ Dropped `rmw_fastrtps_cpp`/`rmw_cyclonedds_cpp` from `rmw-perf.yml` entirely (`PERF_TEST_RMW_IMPLEMENTATIONS` is now `rmw_tickle` only) - Milestone 13's own ~1.5x-of-DDS number, examined more closely, turned out to rest on an unfair comparison, not a real `rmw_tickle` shortfall. Root cause, confirmed by reading `buildfarm_perf_tests`' own `test/test_performance.py.in`: its "two-process" test shape launches both sides via `launch_ros.actions.Node`, which only ever forks local child processes - there is no remote-host launch path in that framework at all, so "two-process" has only ever meant "one host, two OS processes." That structurally favors FastDDS/CycloneDDS, both of which auto-negotiate a same-host shared-memory transport that skips the kernel UDP/IP stack entirely - a transport class `rmw_tickle` doesn't have and structurally can't benefit from in this specific test topology, being built around a real network medium (`hal_linux.c`'s plain UDP sockets) rather than intra-host IPC. Confirmed this isn't fixable by test configuration: `buildfarm_perf_tests/test/CMakeLists.txt`'s `PERF_TEST_RMW_IMPLEMENTATIONS` is a CMake `CACHE` variable overriding `get_available_rmw_implementations()`'s own auto-detected default, so scoping it to `rmw_tickle` only removes the misleading table columns without needing to uninstall the DDS vendors from the `tickle-perf` runner. `rmw-perf.yml`/`README-rmw-perf.md`/`rmw_perf_summary.py` all updated to describe this rig as `rmw_tickle`'s own before/after regression tracker (still genuinely useful for that - it already caught two real wire-level bugs, Milestone 12), not a cross-vendor comparison. **Searched for a more credible alternative** (the request that prompted this): the official body most likely to have one - OSRF/the ROS 2 TSC's own [`TSC-RMW-Reports`](https://github.com/osrf/TSC-RMW-Reports), published every release to help the TSC pick the default `rmw` - turns out to run on `buildfarm_perf_tests` too ("Build Farm Performance Data"), and states its own "simplified network under optimal conditions" caveat plainly, i.e. the identical same-host limitation. No genuinely-cross-host, broadly-adopted rmw benchmark exists upstream; the one tool found that specifically targets it, [`hal-lab-u-tokyo/ros2-perf-multihost`](https://github.com/hal-lab-u-tokyo/ros2-perf-multihost) (REST-orchestrated multi-host runs, `chrony` clock sync), is a single-university-lab research tool with no `rmw_tickle` support and materially less adoption than `buildfarm_perf_tests` or the TSC report - not a credible drop-in replacement. **Conclusion, not yet built**: `ros2/performance_test`'s own `perf_test` binary (what `buildfarm_perf_tests` already wraps) is itself the standard, credible tool - the gap is deployment topology, not tooling. A real cross-host number means running `perf_test` by hand on two separate physical machines (same `ROS_DOMAIN_ID`, `rmw_tickle` given a real subnet via `TICKLE_BROADCAST_ADDR`), the same idea `tickle-hil`'s own Pi rig already applies to native TickLE, just one layer up through `rclcpp`/`rmw` - left as explicit future work, not attempted here | 11, 12, 13 | ✅ done |

| **15** | ✅ Closed Milestone 10's own explicitly-named gap: "explicit xfail/skip list for `rmw_implementation`/`test_rmw_implementation` cases." Preceding research (Milestone 14) already found neither `rmw_fastrtps_cpp` nor `rmw_cyclonedds_cpp` maintains a test suite of its own for this - both point at `ros2/rmw_implementation`'s `test_rmw_implementation` package via `call_for_each_rmw_implementation()`'s auto-discovery, exactly like `rmw_tickle` already registers for via `get_available_rmw_implementations()`. Wired into `check-all.yml` (a new step, not a new self-hosted-runner workflow - `test_rmw_implementation`'s own dependencies are all plain apt-installable ROS 2 packages, unlike `buildfarm_perf_tests`' own two-DDS-vendor underlay), cloning `ros2/rmw_implementation` (`rolling`) + `ros2/test_interface_files` (`rolling`) + `ros2/rcl_interfaces` (`jazzy`, for `test_msgs` - an intentionally mismatched branch pair, the exact one verified end-to-end locally) fresh every run and applying two committed patches (`.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/*.patch`). **Real bugs found and fixed along the way** (not just expected gaps - matching `rmw-perf.yml`'s own Milestone 12 precedent that this kind of integration finds real problems, not just documents known ones): (a) `rmw_init()`/`rmw_init_options_fini()`/`rmw_init_options_copy()`/`rmw_shutdown()`/`rmw_context_fini()` all `strcmp()`d a caller-supplied `implementation_identifier` with no NULL check - a zero-initialized `rmw_init_options_t`/`rmw_context_t` (part of `rmw`'s own public contract, `rmw_get_zero_initialized_*()`) segfaulted instead of returning `RMW_RET_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; the same unguarded pattern existed at ~40 more call sites across every other `rmw_tickle` source file (`rmw_node.c`, `rmw_publisher.c`, `rmw_subscription.c`, `rmw_client.c`, `rmw_service.c`, `rmw_graph.c`, `rmw_wait_set.c`, `rmw_guard_condition.c`) - fixed uniformly via a new shared `rmw_tickle_identifier_matches()` helper (`rmw_tickle.h`). (b) `rmw_init()`/`rmw_init_options_init()`/`rmw_init_options_copy()`/`rmw_shutdown()`/`rmw_context_fini()` had no double-init/double-fini/shutdown-ordering state tracking at all - calling any of them twice, or finalizing before shutting down, silently succeeded instead of the `RMW_RET_INVALID_ARGUMENT` rmw's own lifecycle contract requires; added a `shutdown` flag to `rmw_tickle_context_impl_t` and the missing guards. Fixing this *properly* (freeing `init_options->enclave` in `rmw_init_options_fini()`, previously a plain leak) surfaced a genuine double-free: `rmw_context_fini()` was *also* calling `rmw_init_options_fini()` on `context->options` - a shallow, non-owning copy of the caller's own `options` struct (`context->options = *options` in `rmw_init()`) - so a caller that (correctly, per the same contract) separately finalizes their own `options` after `rmw_context_fini()` would free the same `enclave` pointer twice; fixed by making `rmw_context_fini()` stop finalizing `context->options` at all, since it never owned it. (c) `rmw_create_node()` never validated `name`/`node_namespace` (`rmw_validate_node_name()`/`rmw_validate_namespace()`, both existing `rmw` utilities, simply never called) nor checked whether `context` had already been `rmw_shutdown()`n - either gap let a call the caller expected to fail actually create a real node instead, consuming Milestone 2's one-node-per-process slot and wedging every later test in the same process ("rmw_tickle supports only one node per process for now" cascading across unrelated tests was the actual symptom that led here). (d) `rmw_publisher_event_init()`/`rmw_subscription_event_init()` already correctly returned `RMW_RET_UNSUPPORTED`, but `rmw_borrow_loaned_message()`/`rmw_return_loaned_message_from_publisher()`/`rmw_publish_loaned_message()`/`rmw_take_loaned_message()`/`rmw_take_loaned_message_with_info()`/`rmw_return_loaned_message_from_subscription()` didn't exist as symbols at all - `rmw_implementation`'s own dispatch layer calling through an unresolved (NULL) function pointer segfaulted instead of the test's own `RMW_RET_UNSUPPORTED`-expecting `GTEST_SKIP()` guard ever getting a chance to fire; added all six as real stubs (same "the symbol has to exist even if unimplemented" pattern `rmw_publisher_event_init()` itself already established). (e) `rmw_qos_profile_check_compatible()` (a public `rmw` utility for checking whether two arbitrary QoS profiles work together, independent of `rmw_tickle`'s own accepted-profile subset) didn't exist at all - implemented a real DDS-style request/offer compatibility check (reliability, durability, deadline, liveliness - all four policies rmw_tickle's own QoS roadmap tracks), correctly treating `RMW_DURATION_UNSPECIFIED` (`{0,0}`) as "no constraint" rather than a literal zero-length duration. (f) `tools/typesupport/tickle_typesupport/emit.py` had two real code-generator bugs, found via `test_msgs` (this milestone's own dependency) failing to generate at all: a `bool` constant's default value was spliced in as Python's `True`/`False` instead of C's `true`/`false` (invalid C, `enum { X = True }`); a string default/constant value containing a literal `"` or `\` was spliced into its surrounding C string literal completely unescaped, breaking that literal's own quoting (and the *next* one after it) - both fixed via a new shared `_c_string_literal()` escape helper. **Confirmed-legitimate skips** (the actual gaps, already documented elsewhere in this plan, not new ones): `test_event.cpp` (event support genuinely unimplemented), `TestPublisher`/`TestPublisherUse` in `test_publisher.cpp` and `test_duration_infinite.cpp` (both build a publisher with `rmw_qos_profile_default`, i.e. `RELIABLE` - rmw_tickle only accepts `BEST_EFFORT` for topics, QoS roadmap #5) - all three via the same `rmw_get_implementation_identifier()`-gated `GTEST_SKIP()` pattern upstream already uses for `rmw_connextdds`. `test_service.cpp`/`test_client.cpp` (need `.srv` typesupport, `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp`'s own deliberately-deferred first-cut gap) and `test_wait_set.cpp`/`test_graph_api.cpp`/`test_unique_identifiers.cpp` (same - all three `#include` the `.srv`-generated header even though none of their own tests need a real service) don't compile against `rmw_tickle` at all yet - commented out of `test_rmw_implementation`'s own `CMakeLists.txt` rather than built-and-skipped, since the failure there is at compile time. `test_subscription.cpp`/`test_serialize_deserialize.cpp` needed `test_msgs`' own `Nested`/`MultiNested`/`Arrays`/`WStrings`/`BoundedPlainSequences`/`BoundedSequences`/`UnboundedSequences` messages (nested types, arrays of nested types, arrays of strings - all already-documented gaps in `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`'s own "Supported subset"), excluded from `test_msgs`' own generation the same way `README-rmw-perf.md`'s own provisioning already excludes `Struct256.msg` for the identical reason. **Remaining, not chased down this pass**: `rmw_publisher_count_matched_subscriptions()`/`rmw_publisher_get_matched_subscription_count()`/`rmw_publisher_wait_for_all_acked()` and likely more of the newer/optional `rmw` API surface (`rmw_take_dynamic_message()`, `rmw_feature_supported()`, `rmw_serialization_support_init()`, `rmw_*_set_on_new_*_callback()`, `rmw_*_get_network_flow_endpoints()`, ...) don't exist as symbols either - `rmw_implementation`'s own dispatch tolerates a missing one (unlike the loan functions, which crash) by returning a generic error rather than dereferencing a NULL pointer, so these didn't block this pass, but are real gaps for a future one. (g) `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`'s and `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp`'s own CMake extensions (`cmake/rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c{,pp}_generate_interfaces.cmake`) reconstructed a message's *original* `.msg`/`.srv` source path (needed because `tickle_typesupport` has no OMG IDL parser and can only read the original `.msg`/`.srv` text, not the adapted `.idl` `rosidl_generate_interfaces_ABS_IDL_FILES` actually holds) by guessing `CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR/<subfolder>/<name>.<ext>` - correct only for a package's *own* local message files, and silently wrong for `test_msgs` specifically, which passes in every message except its own `Builtins.msg` from a *different* package's share directory (`test_interface_files`). Every one of `test_msgs`' messages got skipped-with-warning, `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`'s target was never created, and `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp`'s own (correct) check that the target exists failed with an error that named "the `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c` extension must be executed before" - misleadingly ordering-shaped, but the real bug was upstream of ordering entirely. Cost two full wrong-hypothesis CI round trips (moving `find_package()` calls around, since the error text pointed at extension *ordering*) before actually reading the CI log's own "skipping ... only .msg/.srv are supported" warnings closely enough to notice they fired for messages that plainly exist. Also could not be reproduced locally: an earlier debugging session had left hand-created local `.msg` copies sitting in the local dev workspace's own `test_msgs/msg/`, which the broken guess happened to match by accident - a real, upstream `git clone --branch jazzy rcl_interfaces` never has these. Fixed properly by having both extensions look up the adapted `.idl` file's own relative path inside `rosidl_generate_interfaces()`'s own internal `_non_idl_tuples` variable (each entry `"<abs_base_path>:<relative_path>"`, pre-adaptation) - accessible because `rosidl_generate_interfaces()` is a CMake *macro*, not a function, so its internal variables are still live in the calling package's own scope when an `ament_execute_extensions()`-invoked extension file runs. Verified against real, unmodified upstream clones this time (not the tainted local workspace): `test_msgs` now actually generates `.c`/`.cpp` typesupport for `BasicTypes`/`Constants`/`Defaults`/`Empty`/`Strings`, and CI (`check-all.yml` run 35045301504) confirms `test_rmw_implementation` fully green: `test_init_shutdown__rmw_tickle`, `test_init_options__rmw_tickle`, `test_create_destroy_node__rmw_tickle`, `test_publisher__rmw_tickle`, `test_publisher_allocator__rmw_tickle`, `test_subscription_allocator__rmw_tickle`, `test_qos_profile_check_compatible__rmw_tickle`, `test_duration_infinite__rmw_tickle`, `test_event__rmw_tickle` - 9/9, 100% passed, 0 failed. Verified: full local pass (`colcon test --packages-select test_rmw_implementation --ctest-args -R "_rmw_tickle$"`) - 9/9 green (5 pass outright, 3 fixture-skip cleanly, 1 - `test_publisher` - passes after (a)-(d) above); `make test`/`make test-all`/`rmw_tickle`'s own `colcon test` all still pass after every fix; **and now confirmed green in real CI too, not just locally** | 10, 14 | ✅ done |

| **16** | ✅ `.srv` support for `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp`, closing the gap Milestone 11 deliberately deferred and Milestone 15 named as the reason `test_service.cpp`/`test_client.cpp`/`test_wait_set.cpp`/`test_graph_api.cpp`/`test_unique_identifiers.cpp` couldn't even compile against `rmw_tickle`. New `resource/srv__type_support.cpp.in` mirrors `msg__type_support.cpp.in`'s own "pure delegation shim" design one level up: a service's own Request/Response are each an ordinary message in `rosidl_generator_c`'s eyes, so `cmake/rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp_generate_interfaces.cmake`'s extended loop reuses the *same* msg template for each (just with `IDL_NAME` suffixed `_Request`/`_Response`), then generates one more file tying them into a `rosidl_service_type_support_t` via `ROSIDL_TYPESUPPORT_INTERFACE__SERVICE_SYMBOL_NAME` - `.data` borrowed directly from `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`'s own already-generated service handle (matching `rmw_tickle_get_service_callbacks()`'s own C-only lookup, `src/rmw_typesupport.c`), `.request_typesupport`/`.response_typesupport` pointed at the sibling CPP-level Request/Response handles (matching real `rosidl_typesupport_fastrtps_cpp`'s own `srv__type_support.cpp.em` convention, confirmed by reading it directly from a real ROS 2 install rather than guessing). One real gotcha found while writing this, not obvious from the C-level extension's own analogous code: `rosidl_generator_c` emits *one* header per `.srv` (`<name>__functions.h`, covering both `_Request`/`_Response` structs) - the same "strip the suffix before deriving the header path" fix Milestone 1(c)'s own `ros2_header_path()` already needed, applied here to `HEADER_NAME`, computed from the base `_idl_name` before any suffix is appended. New `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_tests/test/test_dispatch_srv_cpp.cpp` closes the exact blind spot Milestone 11's own note named ("the cross-vendor test needs the service-side counterpart of this same C++-reachability fix") - starts from `rosidl_typesupport_cpp::get_service_type_support_handle<T>()` (the real entry point `rclcpp::Client<T>`/`Service<T>` use), unlike `test_dispatch_srv.c`'s own C-only `ROSIDL_GET_SRV_TYPE_SUPPORT()` macro. Also required `.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/test_msgs_rmw_tickle.patch` to stop excluding `test_interface_files_SRV_FILES` from `test_msgs`' own generation (filtered the same way as the `.msg` exclusion list: `Arrays.srv` uses arrays of nested message types, the same unsupported grammar as the excluded `.msg` files, so only it is dropped - `BasicTypes.srv`/`Empty.srv` are both flat/plain-type and kept), and `.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/rmw_implementation_rmw_tickle.patch` to stop commenting out the five executables above (all five only need `test_msgs/srv/basic_types.h`, confirmed by grepping their own `#include`s - none need `Arrays.srv`). `test_subscription.cpp`/`test_serialize_deserialize.cpp` remain excluded (still need nested/array-of-message-type `.msg` support, an unrelated gap). Verified locally first (a real gap found this way, not chased down until it surfaced here): building against a real ROS 2 install with the default static-library CMake config passed a *stale* `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp__identifier` pointer-identity check in the *pre-existing* `test_dispatch_cpp.cpp` too (not something this milestone broke) - `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp`'s own identifier library, built as a plain static archive by CMake's own default, gets a separate copy embedded in every per-interface-package shared library that links it, so the same named external constant has a different address in each; resolved by rebuilding with `-DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=ON`, exactly matching `check-all.yml`'s own existing flag (Milestone 1(c)'s own note on why that flag exists at all) - not a rmw_tickle bug, a test-rig setup gap on this pass's own side, fixed before drawing any conclusion from local results. With that fix, all four `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_tests` dispatch executables (`test_dispatch`, `test_dispatch_srv`, `test_dispatch_cpp`, `test_dispatch_srv_cpp`) pass locally against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install. **Real bugs found once the five restored executables actually ran** (not just compiled - matching this whole plan's own precedent that enabling a real test finds real problems): (a) `rmw_create_service()`/`rmw_create_client()` never validated `service_name` beyond a plain NULL check - an empty string, a name containing spaces, or a relative (no leading `/`) name all silently succeeded instead of being rejected (`test_service.cpp`/`test_client.cpp`'s own `create_with_bad_arguments`) - the same class of gap Milestone 15 already fixed for `rmw_create_node()`'s own name/namespace; fixed by calling the existing `rmw_validate_full_topic_name()` utility, mirroring `rmw_node.c`'s own two-step validate-then-check-result pattern. (b) `rmw_destroy_wait_set()` and `rmw_service_server_is_available()` both returned `RMW_RET_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL argument - matches `rmw/rmw.h`'s own doc comment, but `TestWaitSet.rmw_destroy_wait_set`/`TestClientUse.service_server_is_available_bad_args` both expect `RMW_RET_ERROR` instead; confirmed this isn't a rmw_tickle mistake but a stale doc comment by reading real `rmw_fastrtps_cpp` source (`rmw_fastrtps_shared_cpp/src/rmw_wait_set.cpp`'s own `RMW_CHECK_ARGUMENT_FOR_NULL(wait_set, RMW_RET_ERROR)`) - fixed both to match the real, test-agreeing convention. (c) A significant, previously-unknown architectural gap, found while chasing `TestService.send_reponse_with_bad_arguments`/`send_reponse_with_client_gone`'s own `rmw_wait()` timing out on a same-node client→service round trip that should have succeeded: TickLE's own `process_packet()` (`src/tickle.c`) unconditionally drops any packet whose `header->source` equals the receiving node's own id ("Self sent message") - correct for topic pub/sub (a node already has its own published data locally, no need to hear its own broadcast back), but this makes a client and a service *co-located on the same `tt_Node`* - the only topology Milestone 2's one-node-per-process model allows - structurally unable to ever talk to each other over TickLE's wire protocol: the client's own request datagram gets discarded by this exact filter before the co-located service ever sees it. Confirms and sharpens Milestone 4's own "no live two-process client/server round trip test exists yet" caveat - the real problem turns out to be worse, a single-process client can't even reach its own co-located service. **Not fixed here** (a TickLE-core routing design question - does self-sent suppression need to be scoped per-submessage-type, e.g. skipped for `tt_CLIENT_CALLBACK`/`tt_SERVER_CALLBACK` traffic while staying in place for topic pub/sub - deserves its own dedicated pass, not a rushed change buried in this milestone); the two affected tests are skipped with a comment citing this exact root cause, not just "documented gap" - real follow-on work, tracked here. (d) `TestGraphAPI` (needs a second node in one process, Milestone 2's own deferred limit), `TestWaitSetUse` (needs a working `rmw_subscription_event_init()`/BEST_EFFORT-rejecting subscription, Milestone 11's event gap plus QoS roadmap #5), `TestUniqueIdentifierAPI` and both fixtures derived from it (need a `RELIABLE` publisher, QoS roadmap #5), and `TestService`/`TestClient`'s own `check_qos` (needs a non-default deadline/lifespan/liveliness_lease_duration all at once, QoS roadmap #2/#3/#6) are all confirmed-legitimate skips - every one traces to an already-documented gap elsewhere in this plan, added to `.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/rmw_implementation_rmw_tickle.patch` the same `rmw_get_implementation_identifier()`-gated `GTEST_SKIP()` way as `test_event.cpp`/`test_publisher.cpp` already do. Verified locally: `rmw_tickle`'s own `test_qos`/`test_node_lifecycle`/`test_guard_condition_wait` (Milestone 10) still pass unchanged after (a)/(b) above, and `rmw_service.c`/`rmw_client.c`/`rmw_wait_set.c`/`rmw_graph.c` all compile cleanly against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install. (e) One more real bug, found only in real CI, not locally: `GTEST_SKIP()` inside a fixture's own `SetUp()` is just a `return` statement - it correctly skips the test body when gtest calls that `SetUp()` directly, but `TearDown()` still runs regardless, and every one of (d)'s newly-skipped fixtures had a `TearDown()` that unconditionally destroyed a resource `SetUp()` never got to create (`other_node`, `pub`/`client`, `event`/`client`/`srv`/`sub`/`gc`) - turning a clean skip into a spurious failure; guarded each with the same identifier check. A second layer of the same bug: `TestUniqueIdentifiersForMultiplePublishers`/`Clients` call `TestUniqueIdentifierAPI::SetUp()` as a plain (non-virtual-dispatch) function, so the base's own `GTEST_SKIP()` only returns from *that* call, not from the derived override - execution continued into `rmw_create_publisher(nullptr, ...)` and failed for real; fixed by repeating the identical skip check at the top of both derived `SetUp()`s too. **Confirmed green in real CI**: `check-all.yml` run `35054681478` - `test_rmw_implementation` now runs 14 executables against `rmw_tickle` (up from 9), 100% passed, 0 failed, including all five this milestone restored (`test_wait_set`, `test_graph_api`, `test_unique_identifiers`, `test_service`, `test_client`) | 11, 15 | ✅ done |

| **17** | ✅ **TickLE core extension: deferred/asynchronous service responses**, to remove the cost `rmw_service.c`'s own bridge currently pays - found during a planning-session review of whether `rmw_tickle` correctly implements both of `rmw`'s communication concepts (pub/sub vs client/server), not from a failing test. **The problem, precisely**: `tt_Node_poll()` (`src/tickle.c`) processes exactly one unit of work per call - either the earliest due `scheduler[]` entry (`peek_scheduler()`/`pop_scheduler()`) or one received datagram (`tt_receive()` -> `process_datagram()` -> `drain_rx()`) - then returns to its caller (`rmw_tickle_node_t.poll_thread`'s own loop, Milestone 2). A service request is decoded and answered entirely inside that one call: `build_call_response()` declares `uint8_t response[service->response_size]` as a plain **stack array scoped to that one function call**, invokes `server->callback(server, request, response)` (the `tt_SERVER_CALLBACK` a `struct tt_Server` was created with) synchronously, and encodes+queues the wire response immediately once the callback returns - nothing in TickLE core separates "receive a request" from "eventually answer it" the way `rmw`'s own `rmw_take_request()`/`rmw_send_response()` split expects and requires. `rmw_service.c`'s own `server_callback()` (the actual `tt_SERVER_CALLBACK` `rmw_tickle` installs, Milestone 4) papers over that mismatch by calling `pthread_cond_timedwait(&svc->request_cond, &svc->request_mutex, &deadline)` right there, inside TickLE's own call stack, bounded by `RMW_TICKLE_SERVICE_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_NS` (5s, `rmw_tickle.h`) - which means `tt_Node_poll()` itself cannot return until either `rmw_send_response()` signals it or that timeout elapses, and *nothing else this node owns* (other subscriptions' delivery, other services' requests, pending `call_retry`/`node_update`/`check_liveliness`/`server_cache_clean` scheduler entries) gets a chance to run in the meantime, because the poll loop can't advance to its next iteration while still inside this one. **Why `tt_Node_schedule()` can't fix this from the `rmw_tickle` side alone**: that API only registers a *new, independent, future one-shot* function call (exactly how `call_retry`/`node_update`/`server_cache_clean` already use it) - it has no way to suspend and later resume a call already in progress. Even setting that aside, the `response` buffer `build_call_response()` hands the callback is a raw stack array that stops existing the instant that function returns, so "answer later" isn't expressible through today's `tt_SERVER_CALLBACK` contract at all, independent of any scheduling mechanism - this is a real gap in TickLE core, not a misuse of an existing primitive by `rmw_tickle`. **Proposed fix direction** (not pre-decided - see open questions below): a real deferred-response primitive in TickLE core, per this document's own "Design philosophy" table (absorb an `rmw`-vs-TickLE mismatch into a small, generically-useful TickLE primitive rather than an `rmw`-side-only workaround - the same reasoning Phase 0's `tt_Node_interrupt()` already followed for a different mismatch). Roughly: (a) a new `tt_SERVER_CALLBACK` return sentinel (e.g. `tt_CALL_DEFERRED`, alongside the existing reserved `tt_CALL_TIMEOUT`) telling `build_call_response()` "don't encode/send yet, an answer is coming later"; (b) persisting whatever `build_call_response()` currently only has on the stack (the reply address/sequence context needed to build a `CallResponse` later) into a fixed, no-malloc slot - `struct tt_Server` already has a fixed-capacity, cache-slot mechanism for a related purpose (`cache_buf[tt_MAX_SERVER_CACHE_COUNT][tt_MAX_BUFFER_LENGTH * 2]`/`cache[]`/`clean_config[]`/`clean_scheduled[]`, used today by `resend_cached_response()` to resend an *already-computed* response on a client retry) - worth evaluating whether a "pending, not yet computed" state on that same slot table covers this too, rather than adding a second, parallel fixed array; (c) a new public API, roughly `tt_Server_send_response(struct tt_Server* server, <a request handle from (b)>, struct tt_Response* response)`, callable independently of the call that received the request - from a later `tt_Node_poll()` call on the same poll thread, or (matching `tt_Node_interrupt()`'s own precedent of one narrow, explicitly-documented cross-thread exception to this file's "single thread only" rule) from a different thread entirely, which is what `rmw_send_response()` actually needs, since a ROS service handler can run on whatever thread the executor uses. With that primitive, `rmw_service.c`'s `server_callback()` would convert the incoming request and mark it available exactly as today, then **return `tt_CALL_DEFERRED` immediately** instead of blocking - `tt_Node_poll()` returns right away and the poll thread goes on to service everything else; whenever `rmw_send_response()` is later called, it calls the new `tt_Server_send_response()` directly instead of just setting a flag and signalling a condvar, sending the real answer out with no poll-thread involvement at all. **Open design questions for whoever implements this - deliberately not pre-decided here**: (1) *locking/threading* - `tt_Server_send_response()` being callable from a non-poll thread is new territory beyond `tt_Node_interrupt()`'s own narrow "just wake a blocked `poll()`/`select()` call" scope, since this one needs to *write* into node-owned TX/encode state (`node->tx_buffer` et al.) from that other thread - `DESIGN.md`'s "Concurrency" section (`struct tt_Node` has no internal locking by design, PR #11/#13's add-then-revert history) means this needs either a real, deliberately-scoped lock around just the encode+send path, or requiring the caller to marshal the actual send back onto the poll thread itself (e.g. a small "pending sends" queue `tt_Node_poll()` drains at the top of each call, alongside the scheduler) - the latter keeps TickLE's own no-new-locking philosophy intact but adds a new queue instead; (2) *capacity* - how many deferred/in-flight requests a single `tt_Server` should support concurrently (bounded by `tt_MAX_SERVER_CACHE_COUNT` if slot reuse from (b) is chosen) - `rmw_tickle` can keep enforcing "one outstanding request at a time" at its own layer initially (matching `rmw_tickle_client_t`'s own existing single-outstanding-call limit, a separate already-documented gap this milestone should *not* also try to fix, to keep scope to one deliverable per this document's own established pattern) even if TickLE core itself ends up supporting more; (3) *timeout/cleanup* - what reclaims a deferred slot if the ROS handler never calls `rmw_send_response()` at all, so it doesn't leak forever - likely a `tt_Node_schedule()`-based timer per deferred slot, the same pattern `server_cache_clean` already establishes, replacing today's `RMW_TICKLE_SERVICE_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_NS` `rmw`-side wait with an equivalent TickLE-side one; (4) *retry interaction* - `resend_cached_response()`'s cache-hit path must not re-invoke `server->callback()` a second time for a retry that arrives while the first call is still deferred (only for one that arrives after a real cached response already exists) - needs the "pending" state from (b) checked before falling through to a fresh `build_call_response()` call. **Verification bar**, matching every other milestone in this document: new TickLE-core-level tests in `tests/` proving the deferred-then-answered and deferred-then-timed-out paths directly, with no `rmw_tickle` involved at all (the same "prove the primitive itself first" precedent Phase 0's `test_node_interrupt.c`/`test_liveliness.c`/`test_discovery.c` set), a `DESIGN.md` update (a new subsection alongside "Concurrency: single-threaded per node, by design (for now)"), *then* `rmw_service.c`'s `server_callback()`/`rmw_send_response()` switched over to use it in place of the current `pthread_cond_timedwait()` bridge - real CI proof follows the same "compile+link" → "`colcon test`" → "`test_rmw_implementation` conformance" depth progression every milestone through 16 already established.

**Resolution, decided and implemented**: (1) *locking* - the "pending sends queue drained by the poll thread" option, not a new lock: `tt_Server_send_response()` (any thread) only ever touches `slot_state[]` (via GCC/Clang `__atomic_*` builtins - deliberately not a C11 `_Atomic`-qualified field, since `tickle.h` has to stay includable from C++ and `<stdatomic.h>` isn't a C++ header) and that one slot's own `pending_response_buf[]`/`pending_request_id[]`, never `node->tx_buffer` - the real CDR encode and send happen only on the poll thread, in a new `flush_pending_responses()` called at the top of every `tt_Node_poll()` (same "drain what's ready first" spirit as `drain_rx()`), then a `tt_Node_interrupt()` (Phase 0's own primitive, reused rather than inventing a second one) wakes a poll thread that's currently blocked so it notices promptly. (2) *capacity/one-at-a-time* - `struct tt_Server` reuses its own existing `tt_MAX_SERVER_CACHE_COUNT` slot count (a new `slot_state[]`/`pending_*[]` set of parallel arrays, not folded into the pre-existing `cache[]`/`cache_buf[]` - those two serve genuinely different purposes, *before* vs *after* an answer exists, and forcing them into one dual-purpose structure seemed likely to be more confusing than a second small array set); `rmw_tickle_service_t` still enforces exactly one outstanding request at a time at its own layer, now explicitly (`server_callback()` rejects a second request outright) rather than as a side effect of the old design's blocking. (3) *timeout* - a new `tt_SERVER_DEFERRED_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT` (5s, matching the old `RMW_TICKLE_SERVICE_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_NS` it replaces) reclaims an unanswered slot via `tt_Node_schedule()`, the same pattern `server_cache_clean()` already established. (4) *retry* - `find_pending_slot()` suppresses a retry against a still-deferred request, checked before `process_callrequest()` would otherwise fall through to a fresh callback call.

`tt_SERVER_CALLBACK` gained a new sentinel return value (`tt_CALL_DEFERRED`) and a trailing `tt_RequestId request_id` parameter - a real, deliberate breaking change to a pre-1.0 API, updated in every existing callback (SetBool/ping_pong examples, both Linux and FreeRTOS). New `tests/test_process_callrequest.c` cases prove the deferred-then-answered, deferred-then-timed-out, and retry-while-deferred paths directly at the TickLE-core level, with no `rmw_tickle` involved - including confirming the deferred-then-answered test genuinely catches a regression (reverting the fix locally and re-running made it fail, not just pass by construction). A `DESIGN.md` subsection ("Deferred service responses: a second, narrower exception to 'single-threaded per node'") documents the design, alongside the existing "Concurrency" section it deliberately doesn't reopen.

`rmw_service.c`'s own `server_callback()`/`rmw_send_response()` are switched over: `server_callback()` converts the request and returns `tt_CALL_DEFERRED` immediately instead of blocking; `rmw_send_response()` converts the ROS response and calls `tt_Server_send_response()` directly. `rmw_tickle_service_t` drops `request_cond`/`response_ready` (nothing waits on anything anymore) and gains `pending_request_id`.

**Confirmed in real CI, closing the loop on Milestone 16's own finding**: with both the self-sent-packet-filter fix (Milestone 16, point (c)) and this milestone's deferred-response primitive in place, `test_rmw_implementation`'s own `TestService.send_reponse_with_bad_arguments`/`send_reponse_with_client_gone` - the exact two tests Milestone 16 had to skip, needing a real client→service round trip on one co-located node - now pass for real (`check-all.yml` run `35081147702`, 100% tests passed, 0 failed, out of 14) - the first time a live `rmw_tickle` request/response round trip has ever been proven end to end, not just compile+link depth. Verified locally first: full `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan)/`platform/freertos` build+lint all pass; `rmw_tickle` builds clean against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install with `BUILD_SHARED_LIBS=ON`, its own `test_qos`/`test_node_lifecycle`/`test_guard_condition_wait`/`test_graph` all still pass; `clang-format`/`clang-tidy` clean on every changed file (one real finding along the way, caught by real CI not locally: `tickle/config.h` became unused in `rmw_tickle.h` once its only user, `tt_SECOND` in the now-removed `RMW_TICKLE_SERVICE_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_NS`, was gone - fixed) | 4 | ✅ done |

Deferred, tracked here rather than solved now: **multiple ROS 2 nodes per process** (Milestone 2
assumes one `tt_Node` per process; a component container wanting several ROS nodes in one process
would need one `tt_Node` - own socket, own poll thread - per node, which works but hasn't been
built or measured).

**RESOLVED** (was: "a co-located client can never reach its own co-located service", found by
Milestone 16, point (c)): TickLE's own `process_packet()` (`src/tickle.c`) used to unconditionally
drop any packet whose `header->source` equalled the receiving node's own id ("Self sent message")
- correct for topic pub/sub, wrong for a client and service sharing one `tt_Node` (the only
topology rmw_tickle's one-node-per-process model allows), since the client's own request datagram
was discarded before the co-located service ever saw it. Fixed by threading a `self_sent` flag
down from `process_packet()` through `process_one_submessage()` into `process_submessage()`
itself, which now only suppresses `tt_SUBMESSAGE_TYPE_UPDATE`/`tt_SUBMESSAGE_TYPE_DATA` (topic
pub/sub - unaffected, still self-filtered exactly as before) while letting
`tt_SUBMESSAGE_TYPE_CALLREQUEST`/`tt_SUBMESSAGE_TYPE_CALLRESPONSE` (RPC) through regardless of
sender, since RPC has no separate in-process delivery path the way a node "already has its own
published data locally" is true for pub/sub - the request/response datagrams are the only path.
New `tests/test_malformed_packets.c` cases (`test_self_sent_callrequest_reaches_server`,
`test_self_sent_data_is_still_ignored`) prove both halves directly at the `process_packet()`
level (whitebox, no `rmw_tickle` involved) - confirmed the first one actually catches the
regression by temporarily reverting the fix and re-running (`expected 1, got 0`), not just that
it passes with the fix in place. Verified: full `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan) still
pass, `clang-format`/`clang-tidy` clean on both changed files.

|---|---|---|---|
| **18** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #5 (RELIABILITY/RELIABLE), topic pub/sub**: fills in the ACKNACK primitive TickLE core already reserved wire fields/constants for (`tt_SUBMESSAGE_TYPE_ACKNACK`, `struct tt_AckNackHeader`, `tt_RELIABLE_DEADLINE`/`tt_RELIABLE_RETRY` - config.h's own "Reserved for a future reliable-QoS release" comment) but never implemented; services/clients already had RELIABLE via `tt_Client_call()`'s own bounded retry, so this closes the last gap in the roadmap's own #5 row. **Wire design**: `tt_AckNackHeader` gained a leading `endpoint_id` field - the only per-entity submessage header without one, since it was never actually used before this milestone (`tt_DataHeader`/`tt_CallRequestHeader`/`tt_CallResponseHeader` all lead with theirs already). A reliable Subscriber (`tt_Subscriber.reliable`, `tickle.h`) tracks a cumulative-ack watermark (`ack_seq_no`) plus a 64-sample out-of-order bitmap (`received_bitmap`) in `process_data()`'s new `update_reliable_ack()`; on a gap it unicasts an ACKNACK straight back to the sender (same "reply straight to the request's own source" pattern `process_callrequest()`'s `CallResponse` already established) and arms a retry (`acknack_retry()`, mirroring `call_retry()`'s own schedule/reschedule/give-up shape) capped at `tt_RELIABLE_RETRY` attempts, after which it skips past the unrecoverable hole rather than wedging forever. A reliable Publisher opts in via a new caller-owned `struct tt_ReliableCache` (same "opt-in, caller-owned, zero cost unless used" convention `struct tt_Discovery`/`tt_Node_set_discovery()` already established, deliberately *not* an unconditional field on every `tt_Publisher` the way `struct tt_Server.cache_buf` is) - `tt_Publisher_publish()` snapshots each sample's raw encoded bytes into it (KEEP_LAST ring, capped at a new `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY`, config.h), and an incoming ACKNACK's `process_acknack()` (`process_submessage()`'s own ACKNACK case, previously a no-op stub) resends whichever requested samples are still cached, verbatim, unicast to the requester - retransmission is purely NACK-reactive, never a proactive per-sample timer, to avoid exhausting `tt_MAX_SCHEDULER_LENGTH` under a deep in-flight window. A RELIABLE Publisher forgoes `tt_Publisher_publish()`'s zero-copy fast path (needs the encoded bytes staged in `tx_buffer` at a known location to snapshot) - a deliberate, documented trade-off, not an oversight. New `tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c` (cache store/evict, in-order/no-ACKNACK, gap-then-close, retry exhaustion/give-up, retransmit-on-ACKNACK, best-effort-Publisher-ignores-ACKNACK) proves the primitive itself first, matching every other TickLE-core milestone's own verification order; `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan)/`platform/freertos` build+lint/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` (bare invocation, no compile database) all pass. **`rmw_tickle` reflection**: `rmw_tickle_validate_qos_profile()` (`rmw_qos.c`) now accepts `RELIABLE` for `RMW_TICKLE_ENTITY_PUBLISHER`/`_SUBSCRIPTION` too, not just services/clients; `rmw_create_publisher()` allocates a `struct tt_ReliableCache` (new `rmw_tickle_publisher_t.reliable_cache` field, allocator-owned, freed in `rmw_destroy_publisher()`) sized from `qos_profile->depth` when RELIABLE is requested - defaulting to `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` when unset, and **rejected outright** (not silently clamped) when an explicit depth exceeds that cap, matching this package's own "Rejects anything outside the currently-supported set explicitly" philosophy; `rmw_create_subscription()` sets `tickle_subscriber.reliable` directly, no allocation needed (ack state is two integers). `rmw_tickle/rmw_tickle/test/test_qos.c` updated - RELIABLE now asserts `RMW_RET_OK` for every entity kind instead of `RMW_RET_UNSUPPORTED` for topics. Verified locally against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install: `colcon build --packages-select rmw_tickle rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c rosidl_typesupport_tickle_cpp` with `BUILD_SHARED_LIBS=ON` builds clean, `colcon test --packages-select rmw_tickle` (`test_qos`/`test_node_lifecycle`/`test_guard_condition_wait`/`test_graph`, all 4) passes, `clang-tidy` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed file (one real finding, fixed: `rmw_publisher.c` needed `<tickle/config.h>` included directly for `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY`, not just reachable transitively via `<tickle/tickle.h>` - the same `misc-include-cleaner` class of finding Milestones 3/4 already hit). **CI performance rig**: `examples/linux/perf/perf_client.c`/`perf_server.c` gained a `-R` flag (mirrors `-B`'s own opt-in pattern in `examples/linux/common/cli_opts.h`/`.c`) exercising this at the TickLE-core level directly, ahead of and independent of the `rmw_tickle` reflection above; `.github/scripts/run_perf.sh` runs a third paired scenario (`reliable`, alongside the existing `latency`/`throughput`/`smallmsg`) and `.github/workflows/performance.yml` tracks its throughput (gating, mirrors the existing best-effort throughput group) and loss_pct (graphed only, not gating - `perf_server.c`'s own drop counter predates this feature and isn't retransmission-aware, so a successfully recovered but out-of-order retransmit still counts as a fresh gap there, the same reason `rtt_mdev` doesn't gate either) | 7, 17 | ✅ done |

**Follow-up, found while validating Milestone 18 harder than "does it compile and pass a clean-link
test": `run_perf.sh` gained `tc`/`netem`-based packet-loss injection (1%/5%/10%, `rpi#1`'s own
egress toward `rpi#2`, needs passwordless `sudo tc` there - `.github/scripts/setup-rpi-tc-sudoers.sh`)
running BEST_EFFORT vs RELIABLE side by side at each level, plus a new one-way delivery latency
stat on `perf_server.c` (`avg_latency_ms`) - together the closest thing to a "reliable QoS latency"
benchmark this rig has. First real run exposed a genuine bug, not a tuning limit: RELIABLE's own
effective `loss_pct` came back *worse* than BEST_EFFORT's at every loss level, which shouldn't be
possible given this rig's RTT is far under `tt_RELIABLE_DEADLINE`'s own auto retry interval and
`tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` (8) comfortably covers it. Root cause: `update_reliable_ack()`'s
exact-match branch advanced `ack_seq_no` without also shifting `received_bitmap`, silently
misaligning every bit still tracking a *different*, still-outstanding gap by one position -
permanently - the moment any earlier gap got filled while later out-of-order samples were already
buffered ahead of it (bit `j` is defined to mean `received(ack_seq_no + j)`, matching
`tt_AckNackHeader`'s own wire convention exactly, but only the bitmap-consuming `while` loop was
ever shifting it, never the first, unconditional increment). On the wire this meant an ACKNACK
would silently ask the Publisher to retransmit an already-received sample while dropping the
genuinely-missing one off the request entirely. Fixed by extracting `advance_ack_seq_no()` (used
by both the exact-match branch and `acknack_retry()`'s own give-up path, which had the identical
bug) that always shifts once on top of the increment before absorbing any further already-confirmed
run; new `tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c` case reproduces the exact shape directly. `perf_server.c`'s
own windowed gap counter (added for these same loss scenarios) had the same missing-realignment
shape plus two more off-by-ones (an overflow branch undercounting by one, `finalize_gap_tracking()`
never counting the watermark position itself) - fixed the same way, validated against a battery of
hand-traced scenarios before applying. Confirmed on real hardware after *both* fixes landed
together: RELIABLE's `loss_pct` now runs far below BEST_EFFORT's at every injected level beyond
the lightest one (5% loss: BEST_EFFORT 6.0%, RELIABLE 1.4%; 10% loss: BEST_EFFORT 10.6%, RELIABLE
1.6% - a ~5-7x reduction, not just "roughly half"), with RELIABLE's own one-way latency rising as loss
increases (BEST_EFFORT's stays flat, since it never waits on anything) - the expected shape for
this feature. A new `loss-percent-benchmark.json`/"Track loss-injection loss_pct history" graph
(not gating - a modest ~500-message sample against `tc`/`netem`'s own random per-packet drops is
expected to be noisy run to run) tracks this comparison over time alongside the job summary's own
per-push table.**

**Second follow-up round: closing the gap to the theoretical model.** RTT is the retry interval,
so with `tt_RELIABLE_RETRY = 3` (4 independent transmission attempts, since ACKNACKs travel
`rpi#2`→`rpi#1` and aren't subject to the loss `tc`/`netem` injects only on `rpi#1`'s own egress)
RELIABLE's true loss should be `p^4` - effectively 0 at every level `run_perf.sh` tests. The prior
round's own 1.4-1.6% numbers were still far above that. Chased it through five more real bugs,
each found by pushing real fixes to `main` and reading back real hardware CI results rather than
reasoning from the code alone:
1. A backlog-cascade bug: once a gap exceeded `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` (8), the give-up mechanism
   could only advance `ack_seq_no` one position per `tt_RELIABLE_RETRY` cycle while new messages
   kept arriving at roughly the same rate, so an initial small gap grew into an unbounded backlog
   of positions already evicted from the Publisher's cache. A first fix attempt (fast-forwarding
   reactively on every far-ahead arrival) measurably did nothing on real hardware - it just pinned
   `ack_seq_no` exactly `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY - 1` behind the latest arrival forever, pre-empting
   its own retry cycle before it could resolve. The working fix moved the bulk-skip
   (`skip_unrecoverable_backlog()`) to only ever run from `acknack_retry()`'s own give-up path,
   after the current watermark's fair attempts were already exhausted - never reactively.
2. `send_acknack()` built its wire bitmap as a bare `~received_bitmap`, inverting all
   `tt_RELIABLE_BITMAP_BITS` (64) bits whenever only a few were set - requesting retransmission of
   every position up to 63 ahead of `ack_seq_no` regardless of whether the Publisher had even sent
   that far yet. The Publisher's cache lookup can't tell "evicted" from "never sent"; both looked
   like `loss_pct`-relevant activity, and a real run showed ~4,000 such requests against only ~500
   total messages sent. Fixed by masking the inverted bitmap to bits at or below the highest bit
   actually confirmed received so far (new `highest_received_bit()`, shared with
   `skip_unrecoverable_backlog()`, which already needed the identical scan).
3. A test-harness shutdown race, not a TickLE-core bug: `perf_client.c` tore its node down the
   instant its own `-d` duration elapsed, and `run_perf.sh` sent `perf_server` `SIGINT` immediately
   after, with `perf_server.c`'s own `-W` cooldown defaulting to 0 - so a message lost in roughly
   the last retry-budget-worth of a run had no time to recover: the Publisher was already gone to
   answer a late ACKNACK. Fixed with a `RELIABLE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SEC` grace period on the client
   (stop sending, keep answering ACKNACKs a while longer) and by actually passing `-W` to
   `perf_server` from the loss-injection scenarios.
4. The real dominant cause, found only after the above three landed and `loss_pct` barely moved:
   `perf_server.c`'s own gap-tracking window (`GAP_WINDOW_BITS`, a plain `uint64_t`) gave a
   recovering gap only ~1.3s before forcibly writing it off as dropped. A dedicated diagnostic (a
   bitset marking every `seq` the application ever actually received, independent of the gap
   tracker) proved every single sample this file ever counted as dropped *had* been delivered -
   RELIABLE's own mechanism was never failing, just occasionally taking a few round-trips longer
   than 64 messages to fully drain a burst of correlated loss (since every new DATA arrival
   re-sends the Subscriber's own ACKNACK while any gap is open, not only its 5ms retry timer).
   Widened the window to a 1024-message (16-word) array with matching bit-manipulation helpers -
   longer than `PERF_DURATION_SEC` itself, so the window can no longer be the bottleneck.
Confirmed on real hardware: RELIABLE's own `loss_pct` is now flat at ~0.2% across 1%/5%/10% tc
loss - independent of loss probability, the signature of a fixed, rare artifact (most likely
discovery-registration startup timing) rather than genuine unrecovered data loss, and as close to
the `p^4` theoretical model as this rig's own test tooling can currently resolve. Every TEMPORARY
diagnostic added during this investigation (extra logging in `tickle.c`'s RELIABLE path, the
seen-seq bitset and `DIAG` printfs in `perf_server.c`, the worst-loss-level debug dump in
`run_perf.sh`) was removed once it had served its purpose - the code that remains is the fixes
above, nothing else. Verified the same way as every other change on this branch: `make
test`/`make sanitize`/`platform/freertos` build/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` locally before every
push, real hardware CI (`Check all`/`Test all`/`Performance Test`/`rmw_tickle performance`) as the
final authority.

| **19** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #4 (DURABILITY/TRANSIENT_LOCAL), topic pub/sub**: the last pub/sub QoS gap on the roadmap, closed. Much smaller lift than #5 (RELIABILITY, Milestone 18) - no new wire submessage type at all, since backlog delivery just unicasts the existing DATA submessage format to a newly-discovered peer. **Design**: `struct tt_DurableCache`/`tt_DurableCacheEntry` (`tickle.h`) - a KEEP_LAST ring of raw encoded submessage bytes, structurally identical to `struct tt_ReliableCache` but deliberately a *separate* type: RELIABILITY and DURABILITY are independent QoS policies (either, both, or neither may be requested for the same Publisher), and reusing "ReliableCache" for a durability-only Publisher would be misleading. `tt_Publisher.durable_cache` (NULL by default, opt-in caller-owned pointer, same convention as `reliable_cache`) - `tt_Publisher_publish()` snapshots into it after every successful send via a new `cache_durable_sample()` helper (extracted alongside a matching `cache_reliable_sample()` purely to keep that function's own cognitive complexity under `clang-tidy`'s threshold once both blocks were inlined together). **Delivery trigger**: `upsert_peer()` now returns `bool` - `true` only when it claims a previously-empty peer slot (a genuinely new-to-the-table `node_id`), `false` on an address refresh; `decode_update_entities()`'s own `TOPIC_SUBSCRIBER` branch checks that return value and calls the new `deliver_durability_backlog()` (same `encode()`/`_tt_memcpy()`/`end_encode()` unicast pattern `process_acknack()`'s own retransmit loop already uses) for a genuinely new peer only - not on every periodic UPDATE refresh. Known, documented trade-off: `process_update()`'s own `forget_peers_from_source()` wipes and re-adds *every* peer from a changed source before `decode_update_entities()` re-processes it, so an unrelated endpoint change on an already-known peer node can also look like "new" to `upsert_peer()` and re-trigger delivery - harmless (redelivering already-seen historical samples to an existing Subscriber is idempotent under TRANSIENT_LOCAL semantics), not incorrect. **Deliberately not extended to services/clients**: `tt_Client_call()` is request/response, not pub/sub, so "retained backlog for a late-joining client" has no conceptual analog there (unlike RELIABILITY, where the existing RPC retry already served as the service-side implementation) - `rmw_tickle_validate_qos_profile()` keeps rejecting TRANSIENT_LOCAL for `RMW_TICKLE_ENTITY_SERVICE_OR_CLIENT` accordingly. New `tests/test_durability_pubsub.c` (cache store/evict, backlog delivery to a newly-discovered subscriber - count, address, and the newest sample's own seq_no, `upsert_peer()`'s own new-vs-refresh return value in isolation, no redelivery on a deduped/unchanged UPDATE, VOLATILE-Publisher-is-a-no-op) proves the primitive itself first, matching every other TickLE-core milestone's own verification order; `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan)/`platform/freertos` build/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` (bare invocation) all pass, no regressions in the existing suite. **`rmw_tickle` reflection**: `rmw_tickle_validate_qos_profile()` (`rmw_qos.c`) now accepts `TRANSIENT_LOCAL` for `RMW_TICKLE_ENTITY_PUBLISHER`/`_SUBSCRIPTION`, still rejects it for `_SERVICE_OR_CLIENT`; `rmw_create_publisher()` allocates a `struct tt_DurableCache` (new `rmw_tickle_publisher_t.durable_cache` field, allocator-owned, freed in `rmw_destroy_publisher()`) sized from `qos_profile->depth` when TRANSIENT_LOCAL is requested - defaulting to `tt_MAX_DURABLE_HISTORY` when unset, and **rejected outright** (not silently clamped) when an explicit depth exceeds that cap, same philosophy as RELIABLE's own depth handling; `rmw_subscription.c` needed **no change at all** - durability delivery is purely a Publisher-side decision in TickLE core's own design, so no field exists on `rmw_tickle_subscriber_t`/`struct tt_Subscriber` for it. `test_qos.c` updated - TRANSIENT_LOCAL now asserts `RMW_RET_OK` for publisher/subscription, `RMW_RET_UNSUPPORTED` kept for service/client. Verified locally against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install: `colcon build --packages-select rmw_tickle` builds clean, `colcon test --packages-select rmw_tickle` passes (8 tests, 0 errors, 0 failures), `clang-tidy` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed file | 7, 18 | ✅ done |

**Follow-up, a one-off HIL experiment (not merged - tried, answered, reverted)**: does enabling
DURABILITY on `run_perf.sh`'s `reliable` loss-injection scenario reduce RELIABLE's own flat ~0.1%
`loss_pct` floor (Milestone 18's own follow-up paragraph above, never conclusively root-caused,
only guessed at as "most likely discovery/registration startup timing")? Hypothesis: DURABILITY's
discovery-triggered backlog push (this milestone) redelivers a Publisher's still-cached samples the
moment it learns of a new Subscriber peer, which could plausibly catch whatever's lost in that early
window. Tested by adding a temporary `-D` flag to `perf_client.c` (mirroring `-R`'s own opt-in
pattern) and a `loss${pct}_reliable_durable` scenario alongside the existing besteffort/reliable
ones, real hardware run via `Performance Test` (commits `9a96cf8`/`ae61610`, both since reverted -
`0bf299b`/`f358b9c`). Result: **no improvement** - `1%`: 0.1/0.1, `5%`: 0.1/0.1, `10%`: 7.9/8.2
(reliable/reliable+durable) - the floor is exactly as present with DURABILITY on, and 10%'s own
number went slightly the *wrong* way (within this rig's own established run-to-run noise, per
Milestone 18's follow-up paragraph, not necessarily a real regression). Consistent with DURABILITY's
own design: its backlog push is a one-shot unicast with no ACK/retry of its own (`deliver_durability_
backlog()`, this milestone), so it faces the exact same tc/netem loss probability as any other
packet and adds no reliability the plain ACKNACK path didn't already have - it was never a
retransmission mechanism, so it had no real reason to plug a loss floor RELIABLE's own retry chain
doesn't already cover. The ~0.1% floor's actual root cause remains unproven; the `-D` CLI flag and
scenario were reverted rather than kept (no ongoing benefit, and it cost real CI minutes each run) -
`-D` itself (the underlying `tt_Publisher.durable_cache` opt-in) can trivially be re-added to
`perf_client.c` later if a different hypothesis wants it.

**Follow-up, `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` widened 8 -> 10 (kept, merged)**: separately from the
DURABILITY experiment above, asked to make `loss1_reliable`/`loss5_reliable` land at (effectively)
0 loss_pct while `loss10_reliable` still shows a little - `run_perf.sh`'s own `LOSS_TEST_INTERVAL_
SEC` comment already established the retained window that matters for RELIABLE's own cache-
eviction-vs-ACKNACK-round-trip race is `depth * send interval`, and that whole investigation only
ever tuned the interval half of that product at a fixed depth of 8. Widened `config.h`'s own
`tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` instead, leaving `LOSS_TEST_INTERVAL_SEC` (280us) untouched so BEST_
EFFORT's own already-tuned loss_pct accuracy (which depends on interval/sample count, not depth)
stays as-is. Two real HIL data points at that same 280us interval: depth 16 (double) overshot -
1%/5%/10% all landed flat at 0.1%, erasing 10%'s own differentiation entirely, the opposite of
what was wanted; depth 10 landed 1%/5% at 0.1% (unchanged) and 10% at 3.6% (real, well under
BEST_EFFORT's own ~9.8% there, and calmer than depth 8's own noisy 5.2%/9.6%/8.0% swings across
identical re-runs) - kept at 10. `tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c`'s `test_acknack_retry_skips_
unrecoverable_backlog_on_giveup` had a seq_no (14) hardcoded against the old depth of 8 to exercise
the "gap wider than tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY" give-up path - parametrized to `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_
HISTORY + 6` so it stays correct at any depth; `make test`/`make sanitize`/`platform/freertos`
build/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` all pass. Not re-run for reproducibility (this whole cliff has
already shown real run-to-run jitter at the interval knob - see `LOSS_TEST_INTERVAL_SEC`'s own
comment - so 3.6% specifically should be read as "confirmed small and real," not a number future
runs will reproduce exactly) - stopped here at the user's own call, not chased further.

| **20** | ✅ **DURABILITY backlog delivery isn't protected by RELIABILITY's own ACKNACK, even when a Publisher has both QoS policies enabled** - found during a planning-session walkthrough of why "just raise `depth` and turn on `TRANSIENT_LOCAL`" doesn't close Milestone 18's own residual ~0.1-0.2% `loss_pct` floor for a late-joining Subscriber, and confirmed by re-reading the already-reverted `reliable+durable` HIL experiment (Milestone 19's own follow-up paragraph, commits `9a96cf8`/`ae61610`, reverted in `0bf299b`/`f358b9c`) rather than a new test run. **What the actual root cause turned out to be, on closer tracing (correcting this row's own original diagnosis)**: `deliver_durability_backlog()`'s own unicast packets are, byte-for-byte, ordinary DATA submessages (verbatim copies of what `tt_Publisher_publish()` originally encoded, headers included) - `process_data()` cannot tell a backlog-delivered sample apart from a live one, so it *already* runs every one of them through `update_reliable_ack()` exactly like any other arrival, no `deliver_durability_backlog()` changes needed at all for that part. The real, single bug was one level down, in `update_reliable_ack()` itself: a gap `>= tt_RELIABLE_BITMAP_BITS` (64 - `tt_AckNackHeader.bitmap`'s own fixed wire width) hit a branch that just logged a warning and left `ack_seq_no` untouched, **permanently** - every later arrival, however perfectly in-order from that point on, had the identical too-wide offset relative to the still-stuck `ack_seq_no`, so a healthy stream never recovered once this fired once. A brand-new Subscriber's default `ack_seq_no` (1) sitting arbitrarily far behind a Publisher that's already been running a while (exactly what a durability backlog implies) reliably triggers this on first contact - matching the reverted HIL experiment's own flat "no improvement" result precisely, without needing `deliver_durability_backlog()` to be at fault the way this row originally assumed. **The fix**: that branch (`src/tickle.c`'s `update_reliable_ack()`) now jumps `ack_seq_no` straight to the new arrival instead of freezing - safe specifically because an offset this wide could *never* have been named in a 64-bit ACKNACK bitmap in the first place, so nothing genuinely recoverable is being given up on (unlike the "far ahead but still inside the tracking window" case this same function's own adjacent comment already warns against fast-forwarding on - a real, different, already-recoverable case that must still resolve through the normal retry cycle). This is the *same* "give up on what's provably unrecoverable" logic `skip_unrecoverable_backlog()` already applies at its own, later trigger point (after retries are exhausted) - applied here the instant it's already known un-trackable, rather than staying stuck forever first. Fixes both the DURABILITY-specific manifestation this row is about *and* a more general, previously-latent RELIABLE-only bug (any single real burst loss wider than 64, durability or not, hit the identical dead end). **Resolves this row's own open "sequencing" question directly**: no, `update_reliable_ack()`'s first-contact behavior did *not* already correctly adopt a fresh baseline - it silently froze instead, which is exactly what needed fixing. **The depth-invariant open question, resolved as a real, enforced constraint** (not just a note): `deliver_durability_backlog()` needs every entry it sends to also be resolvable in `reliable_cache` for the fix above to matter at all (otherwise the Subscriber correctly detects and ACKNACKs the gap, but `process_acknack()` finds nothing cached to answer with) - true today only because `tt_MAX_DURABLE_HISTORY` (8) happens to be `<= tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` (10), since both caches are written together, in lockstep, on every publish (`cache_reliable_sample()`/`cache_durable_sample()`), making a smaller-or-equal `tt_MAX_DURABLE_HISTORY` a guaranteed trailing subset of `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY`'s own retained window. Pinned down with a new `_Static_assert(tt_MAX_DURABLE_HISTORY <= tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY, ...)` (`src/tickle.c`, next to the pre-existing `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY <= tt_RELIABLE_BITMAP_BITS` one) plus cross-referencing doc comments on both `config.h` constants, so a future independent change to either can't silently break this without the build itself refusing to compile - no `rmw_tickle`-side change needed, since ROS 2's own `rmw_qos_profile_t.depth` is a single shared field, structurally unable to request different depths for the two policies in the first place. **Ordering/batching** (this row's own third open question): left as today's behavior (one unicast burst, oldest-first, Subscriber ACKNACK-requests whatever's missing) - the fix above is what makes that already work correctly, no batching/pacing change needed. **New tests**: `tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c`'s `test_reliable_subscribe_oversized_first_gap_jumps_baseline_instead_of_freezing()` reproduces the underlying bug at the plain RELIABLE level (no durability involved) and confirms the stream tracks normally immediately afterward; `tests/test_durability_pubsub.c`'s `test_durability_backlog_recovered_via_acknack_when_reliable_too()` is the actual end-to-end proof this row's own verification bar asked for - a Publisher with both caches populated from a simulated 100-sample stream, a late-joining reliable Subscriber whose first backlog arrival is 96 past the bitmap window, and a *subsequent* backlog sample "lost in flight" that's confirmed both correctly ACKNACK-requested (Subscriber side, `process_data()`/`update_reliable_ack()`) and actually still retransmittable (Publisher side, `process_acknack()` finding it in `reliable_cache`) - both sides simulated directly on one mock node, the same convention `test_reliable_pubsub.c`'s own `process_data()`/`process_acknack()` tests already use. `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan)/`platform/freertos` build/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` (bare invocation) all pass, no regressions in the existing suite. **Not done here** (deliberately, real follow-on work, not silently dropped): re-running the reverted HIL experiment (this row's own verification bar also asked for this) to confirm the `loss_pct` gap actually closes on real hardware, not just in the TickLE-core unit tests above - the `-D`/`loss${pct}_reliable_durable` scenario and its own job-summary table were fully reverted (Milestone 19's own follow-up paragraph) and would need re-adding first; and the still-unconfirmed "discovery/registration startup timing" hypothesis for Milestone 18's own separate ~0.1-0.2% floor (this row's own neighboring paragraph, below) remains exactly as unconfirmed as before - this fix addresses a *different*, now-proven mechanism (the oversized-gap freeze), not that one. | 18, 19 | ✅ done |

**Separately, still unconfirmed**: Milestone 18's own residual ~0.1-0.2% `loss_pct` floor (present even with plain RELIABLE, no DURABILITY involved) was only ever guessed at as "most likely discovery-registration startup timing," never conclusively root-caused - the diagnostic that most directly targeted this shape of bug (`process_data()`'s completely silent `find_endpoint() == NULL` early return in `src/tickle.c`, *before* `update_reliable_ack()` ever runs, so a dropped-here sample is invisible to every part of the reliable-delivery machinery Milestone 18 built) was added and removed while chasing a *different*, since-fixed bug (the gap-tracking-window issue, Milestone 18's own follow-up round #4) and was never re-tested in isolation against the smaller residual floor that remained afterward. A concrete, cheap way to test this specific hypothesis without new instrumentation: `.github/scripts/run_perf.sh`'s own `sleep 1 # let the server bind before the client starts sending` (`run_paired_test()`) is a fixed wait covering SSH connection setup *and* the remote `perf_server` process's own `tt_Node_create()`/`tt_Node_create_subscriber()` - not a real readiness handshake - so widening it (e.g. to 3s) and re-running just the `reliable`/`loss*_reliable` scenarios would confirm or rule this out directly; if the floor shrinks or disappears, the real fix is replacing the blind `sleep` with an actual readiness signal (e.g. `run_paired_test()` polling the server's own log for its `"Node created(#%d)"`/subscriber-created output before starting the client) rather than any TickLE-core change. Note this is very likely a **test-harness timing gap, not a TickLE-core or `rmw_tickle` bug** - real ROS 2 usage essentially always has a Subscriber's node already up before a Publisher starts publishing in earnest, so low real-world urgency even if confirmed; tracked here mainly so it isn't confused with, or used to explain away, this row's own separate DURABILITY/ACKNACK integration gap above (the reverted HIL experiment already shows durability delivery has its own independent problem, on top of whatever this startup-timing question turns out to be). **Update (Milestones 23/24)**: every mechanism tried since that operates on *retained samples* - durability backlog push, discovery-triggered Heartbeat, periodic Heartbeat, and a 6x deeper `reliable_cache` (10 → 64) - has now failed to move this floor at all, real HIL numbers identical (`0.1%`) across every variant. That's real evidence *against* a retention-window explanation specifically, and mild evidence *for* the "silent early drop before anything reliable-related even runs" shape this paragraph's own `find_endpoint() == NULL` hypothesis describes.

**Update 2 - the `find_endpoint() == NULL` diagnostic itself, finally re-tested (this row's own second concrete next step, commits adding then removing a TEMPORARY `TT_LOG_WARNING` at that exact branch, `.github/scripts/run_perf.sh` surfacing the count per tc loss level)**: the phenomenon is real, but doesn't explain the floor either. Across the *whole* HIL run, 738 such drops fired - heavily clustered as sequential runs of low seq_nos (1, 2, 3, 4...) right at the very start of the un-paced `throughput`/`reliable` (firehose, no `-i` pacing) scenarios specifically, exactly the shape a startup race predicts: at full send rate, dozens of samples can go out before the just-SSH-launched `perf_server` process finishes `tt_Node_create_subscriber()`. But the *paced* `loss1_reliable`/`loss5_reliable`/`loss10_reliable` scenarios - the only ones that actually produce the `0.1%` floor this whole investigation is about - saw only **1 / 0 / 0** such drops respectively, nowhere near the ~36 needed to explain `0.1%` of a ~35,714-message run. Worse for the hypothesis: that one `loss1_reliable` occurrence wasn't even in the startup window - it was at **seq_no 9004**, deep into an already-healthy stream, not the same phenomenon at all. **Ruled out** as the floor's explanation, though confirmed real and worth remembering for anyone chasing *firehose*-mode reliability instead (a real, separate, rate-dependent startup-race artifact, distinct from this floor).
