The `sleep 1` widening test above remains untried and is now the more promising concrete next step - or, having eliminated every mechanism operating on retained samples *and* the pre-registration silent-drop path, the remaining candidate space is narrowing toward something in between: a sample that *does* reach an already-registered Subscriber but is lost somewhere `update_reliable_ack()` doesn't yet instrument (e.g. a decode failure, a duplicate-seq_no collision, or genuine rare packet corruption surviving `tc`'s own loss injection - the `seq_no 9004` anomaly above is one concrete, unexplained data point in that direction, not yet itself investigated).

**Resolved (Milestone 25)**: the floor's real cause was never in `tickle.c` at all, nor in anything this row spent so long chasing - it was a measurement bug in `examples/linux/perf/perf_server.c`'s own `track_arrival()`, which was reporting false drops for samples that had genuinely arrived (100% false-positive rate on `loss1_reliable`/`loss5_reliable` before the fix, confirmed both by a standalone simulation and real HIL). Fixed; real HIL `loss1_reliable`/`loss5_reliable` `loss_pct` is now exactly `0.0%`. `loss10_reliable` improved sharply (54 → 17 falsely-reported drops) but still shows a smaller residual gap against ground truth (17 reported vs 3 genuinely missing) - see Milestone 25's own row for the leading hypothesis and next step. The `sleep 1` widening test above is now low-priority: with the measurement tool itself fixed, there's no longer a `0.1%`-sized phantom floor left to explain via a startup race - only `loss10_reliable`'s own small residual remains open, and that one already has a more specific, better-supported lead (`GAP_WINDOW_BITS` still occasionally too narrow under the heaviest loss bursts) than a generic SSH-startup-latency guess.

| **21** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #2 (DEADLINE) + #3 (LIVELINESS)**: closes both remaining pub/sub QoS gaps in one pass - both rows say "`rmw_tickle`"/"Phase 0's mechanism, generalized" (not "TickLE core") and confirmed to need none: `tt_Node_schedule()`/`tt_Node_unschedule()` (already exposed) and `check_liveliness()`'s own existing per-node peer-death detection (`tickle.c`) are sufficient. Scoped to the full `rmw_event_t` API (`rmw_publisher_event_init()`/`rmw_subscription_event_init()` actually returning `RMW_RET_OK` and delivering real counts via a new `rmw_take_event()`, not just relaxing QoS validation) - `rclcpp` has had no way to *observe* either policy until now (`rmw_publisher_event_init()`/`rmw_subscription_event_init()` previously always returned `RMW_RET_UNSUPPORTED`, Milestone 11/15's own stub). **`rmw_qos.c`**: any finite `deadline` now accepted (a purely local timer, no wire cadence to bound it). `liveliness` stays `AUTOMATIC`-only (`MANUAL_BY_TOPIC`/`_BY_NODE` still rejected - no assertion-API model exists), but a custom `liveliness_lease_duration` is now accepted **down to `tt_LIVELINESS_MISS_THRESHOLD * tt_NODE_UPDATE_INTERVAL`** (TickLE core's own fastest possible peer-death detection latency) - rejected explicitly below that floor rather than silently rounding up, matching RELIABLE's/DURABLE's own depth-cap precedent. Both checks apply for every `entity_kind` (unblocks Milestone 16(d)'s own named `check_qos` gap for services/clients too), though the monitoring below is Publisher/Subscription-only (`rmw/event.h` has no service/client deadline/liveliness event at all). **DEADLINE** (`rmw_publisher.c`/`rmw_subscription.c`): new `rmw_tickle_event_status_t {atomic_int total_count, unread_count;}` (`rmw_tickle.h`) - one shared shape for every count this milestone tracks, atomic since a poll-thread-only writer (the scheduled check) and an any-thread reader (`rmw_take_event()`) share it lock-free (`unread_count`'s own atomic_exchange-on-read/atomic_load-on-peek split mirrors `check_guard_conditions()`'s own established `has_triggered` pattern exactly). `rmw_create_publisher()`/`_subscription()` arm a `tt_Node_schedule()` check at `qos.deadline` (if finite) that compares wall-clock time against `last_activity_time` (set by `rmw_publish()`/`subscriber_callback()`, both already running under `node->mutex` - the exact same lock the scheduled check itself runs under, since `tt_Node_schedule()` callbacks fire from inside `tt_Node_poll()`, which `poll_thread` already holds that mutex around) and reschedules itself unconditionally every period (DDS's own "one miss per elapsed period with no write/read" semantics) - each miss also broadcasts the shared `wait_cond` (new `wake_wait_cond()` helper, `rmw_subscription.c`, also absorbing `subscriber_callback()`'s own pre-existing inline version) so a blocked `rmw_wait()` doesn't have to wait out its own unrelated timeout to notice. `rmw_destroy_publisher()`/`_subscription()` `tt_Node_unschedule()` a still-armed check before freeing. **LIVELINESS** (`AUTOMATIC` only): `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_LOST` (Publisher) is real and queryable but **structurally, permanently `{0, 0}`** - TickLE's only local "am I still signaling liveliness" mechanism is the poll thread's own periodic self-announce (`node_update()`), driven by the *same* scheduler loop any self-check would also need to run from, so a miss here is unfalsifiable from inside the same process (if the check executes, the announce already did too) - documented as an honest architectural limit, not a placeholder. `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_CHANGED` (Subscription) is the real signal: generalizes `count_matching()` (`rmw_graph.c`, backing `rmw_count_publishers()`) into "how many Publishers on my topic are alive right now," polled periodically at `liveliness_lease_duration` (or the `rmw_qos.c` floor above, if left `SYSTEM_DEFAULT`) - **but `count_matching()` itself takes `node_impl->mutex`, and this periodic check runs from inside `tt_Node_poll()` with that same (non-recursive) mutex already held by `poll_thread`, so calling it directly would self-deadlock**; fixed by splitting `rmw_graph.c`'s own scan into a lock-free `count_matching_locked()` core, exposed as `rmw_tickle_count_matching_locked()` (`rmw_tickle.h`) for this exact caller, with `count_matching()` itself now just a thin lock-then-call wrapper around it (a real bug this milestone's own design caught before it shipped, not found via testing). `alive_count`/`not_alive_count_change` are real; `not_alive_count` itself (a live snapshot, not a delta) is always reported as `0` - TickLE's own `tt_Discovery` never keeps a tombstone for a peer `check_liveliness()` presumed dead, it's just removed, so there's no "known but currently dead" set to report a real snapshot from (same honest-limitation precedent as `LIVELINESS_LOST` above). `rmw_subscription_event_init()` starts this tracking **lazily** (idempotent - only the first call for this event type arms it), matching this package's established "pay only for what you actually use" convention (`reliable_cache`/`durable_cache`'s own NULL-costs-nothing shape). **`rmw_wait_set.c`**: new `check_events()`/`event_status_for()` following the exact same peek(never mutates)/finalize(nulls non-ready slots) split every other `check_*()` here already uses - unlike a guard condition, an event's readiness is *consumed by `rmw_take_event()`*, not by `rmw_wait()` itself (mirrors `check_subscriptions()`'s own `queue_count > 0` peek, which `rmw_take()` - not `rmw_wait()` - actually drains), so `check_events()`'s finalize branch still only peeks. New `rmw_event.c`: `rmw_take_event()`/`rmw_event_fini()`/`rmw_get_zero_initialized_event()`/`rmw_event_type_is_supported()` - the "read the current status" half, dispatching on `event_handle->event_type` into the matching real `rmw/events_statuses/*.h` struct. New `tests/test_events.c` (well, `rmw_tickle/rmw_tickle/test/test_events.c`) - the **first** `rmw_tickle` test to exercise real `rmw_create_publisher()`/`_subscription()`/`rmw_publish()` end to end (every earlier Milestone 10 test stayed one level below that); needs a real `rosidl_message_type_support_t`, which normally only a generated interface package provides, so it hand-builds the exact same shape a real generated `<name>__type_support.c` produces (confirmed against one from a real build), the same spirit as `test_graph.c`'s own fake `tt_DATA_ENCODE`/`_DECODE` stubs one level up. Covers: a Publisher/Subscription that never publishes/receives really does miss its deadline (via real `rmw_wait()` + `rmw_take_event()`, not a mocked clock - the `wake_wait_cond()` broadcast means this returns in ~60ms, not the full 500ms timeout budget, confirmed stable across repeated local runs); `LIVELINESS_LOST` stays `{0, 0}` even after real time has passed; `LIVELINESS_CHANGED` is a **smoke test only** (`event_init()`/`rmw_take_event()` wiring proven, `alive_count` stays `0` with no matched Publisher ever discovered) - a real alive/not-alive transition needs a *second* node, which Milestone 2's own one-node-per-process limit means this process can't have. `test_qos.c` extended (deadline/lease-duration accept/reject cases, mirroring Milestone 18/19's own pattern). Verified against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install: `colcon build --packages-select rmw_tickle` clean, `colcon test --packages-select rmw_tickle` - 5/5 green (`test_qos`, `test_node_lifecycle`, `test_guard_condition_wait`, `test_graph`, new `test_events`), `test_events` itself re-run 5x directly via `ctest` to confirm stable ~0.20s timing, not flaky; `clang-tidy` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed file (two real findings, fixed: `rmw_event.c` needed direct includes for the four `rmw/events_statuses/*.h` status struct headers rather than the umbrella `events_statuses.h`, and `test_events.c`'s own hand-rolled fake message structs needed renaming to `lower_case` to satisfy this repo's own `.clang-tidy` `StructCase` rule - both `misc-include-cleaner`/`readability-identifier-naming` classes of finding prior milestones already hit elsewhere). | 7, 18 | ✅ done |

| **22** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #5 (RELIABILITY) follow-up: periodic Heartbeat** - closes the one structural piece TickLE's own RELIABLE/DURABILITY still lacked relative to real DDS/RTPS: a Publisher never *announced* what it currently retained, a Subscriber only ever inferred anything was missing reactively from whatever DATA happened to arrive. Two concrete gaps this closes: Milestone 20's own "jump `ack_seq_no` to whatever arrives first" fix is a *workaround* for not having this signal - it guesses the baseline from whichever backlog sample happens to survive in flight, and if *that specific* first arrival is itself lost, the Subscriber never learns the correct baseline at all; and a Subscriber could only ever detect a gap off a *later* DATA arrival - an idle stream (or every remaining packet also lost) meant the gap was silently never noticed at all, `acknack_retry()`'s own timer never even getting armed. **Wire design**: new `tt_SUBMESSAGE_TYPE_HEARTBEAT` (6) + `struct tt_HeartbeatHeader {endpoint_id, first_available_seq_no, last_seq_no}` (`tickle.h`) - RTPS's own HEARTBEAT submessage, one level simpler (no separate count/liveliness fields RTPS's own version carries, not needed here). **Publisher side**: new `tt_Publisher.heartbeat_period_ns` field (0 = disabled) + `tt_Publisher_set_heartbeat_period()` (an explicit call, not a passive field write, since arming a periodic `tt_Node_schedule()` entry is an active operation with no `reliable_cache`/`durable_cache`-style passive-pointer equivalent; requires `reliable_cache` already set - rejected outright otherwise, nothing to announce for a best-effort Publisher). `send_heartbeat()` derives `[first_available_seq_no, last_seq_no]` straight from `reliable_cache`'s own current ring state (a small scan, depth ≤ `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY`) and `pub->seq_no` - no new cache needed - reusing `tt_Publisher_publish()`'s own peer/broadcast decision; skips sending (but still reschedules) while nothing has been published yet. `tt_Publisher_destroy()` cancels a still-armed Heartbeat, the first Publisher-side scheduled timer to need this. **Subscriber side**: new `tt_Subscriber.reliable_heartbeat_last_seq_no` field (0 = no Heartbeat seen yet, today's exact behavior when a Publisher never enables one) + `process_heartbeat()` (`process_submessage()`'s new case, `self_sent`-guarded like `ACKNACK`'s own). On first-ever reliable contact (`reliable_sender_node_id == tt_NODE_ID_INVALID`, the same sentinel `send_acknack()` already uses) sets `ack_seq_no` straight from `first_available_seq_no` - the actual DDS-parity fix, learning the *real* starting point independent of whether any specific backlog sample's own delivery attempt succeeded, unlike Milestone 20's own guess-from-first-arrival workaround. An already-tracking Subscriber whose Heartbeat reveals a gap `>= tt_RELIABLE_BITMAP_BITS` reuses Milestone 20's own jump-ahead logic, now factored into a shared `jump_ack_baseline()` helper (called from both `update_reliable_ack()`'s own oversized-DATA-gap branch and here) rather than duplicated. A stale/reordered Heartbeat (`last_seq_no < ack_seq_no`, real risk over UDP) is a no-op, and `reliable_heartbeat_last_seq_no` itself is monotonic-guarded so a late, older Heartbeat can never regress what's already known. New shared `highest_relevant_bit()` widens both `send_acknack()`'s own request range and `maybe_arm_acknack_retry()`'s own "is there anything to do" gate beyond `received_bitmap`'s own highest *confirmed* bit alone, to also reach as far as the most recent Heartbeat's own `last_seq_no` - the actual mechanism that lets a Heartbeat trigger a real ACKNACK cycle even with zero out-of-order DATA arrivals yet. **New `tests/test_heartbeat.c`** (9 tests, mirrors `tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c`'s own whitebox conventions): `tt_Publisher_set_heartbeat_period()`'s own reliable_cache requirement and arm/disarm scheduling; `send_heartbeat()` deriving the announced range correctly (including after eviction has moved the oldest retained entry forward) and correctly skipping an empty cache; the actual DDS-parity case - a brand-new Subscriber learning its baseline and immediately triggering a real ACKNACK purely from a Heartbeat, zero DATA ever received; an already-tracking Subscriber's oversized-gap jump *and* its within-window widening (two different Heartbeat-revealed-gap shapes, both correct); a best-effort Subscriber correctly ignoring a Heartbeat entirely; `tt_Publisher_destroy()` cleanly cancelling an armed Heartbeat schedule entry (`node.scheduler_tail` observed directly). `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan)/`platform/freertos` build/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` (bare invocation) all pass, no regressions in the existing suite (including Milestone 20's own two tests, still green using the newly-shared `jump_ack_baseline()`). **Not done here** (deliberately, per this session's own established "core first" ordering - same scoping note as `DURABILITY`'s own plan): `rmw_tickle`/`examples/linux/perf` wiring (a QoS-profile-driven or CLI-flag-driven heartbeat period) - DDS itself doesn't expose Heartbeat period as a portable, standard `DataWriterQos` field either, so this needs its own separate design pass on what (if anything) `rmw_tickle` should expose; re-deriving `deliver_durability_backlog()` on top of this (a Heartbeat + ACKNACK cycle could, in principle, let a newly-discovered Subscriber pull its own backlog reactively instead of the Publisher proactively pushing it) - a real, larger architectural simplification worth a future look, not bundled into this already-substantial change. | 18, 20 | ✅ done |

| **23** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #5 (RELIABILITY) follow-up: discovery-triggered immediate Heartbeat** - Milestone 22's own periodic Heartbeat can't close Milestone 18's own residual ~0.1% `loss_pct` floor even in principle: it wasn't wired into `examples/linux/perf/perf_client.c` at all yet (deliberately deferred, Milestone 22's own "Not done here"), and even once wired (via a temporary `-H` flag, below), any *practical* period is necessarily far longer than `reliable_cache`'s own real-time retention window, so it can only ever arrive well after the affected early samples are already evicted. Proposed fix (reframed from the user's own "have the Subscriber ACKNACK right after it's created" idea - mechanically backwards, since a Subscriber can't usefully ACKNACK before it knows the Publisher's address/valid range at all): have the **Publisher** send one immediate, unsolicited Heartbeat the instant `decode_update_entities()`'s own `upsert_peer()` claims a previously-empty slot for a genuinely new Subscriber peer - mirroring `deliver_durability_backlog()`'s own identical trigger exactly, firing within the few-ms discovery-completion window itself rather than waiting on any periodic schedule. **Implementation**: `reliable_cache_oldest_seq_no()` and the wire-encoding half of `send_heartbeat()` extracted into shared `encode_and_send_heartbeat(node, pub, first_seq_no, peers, peer_count)` (explicit target, no more internal broadcast-vs-unicast decision duplicated); new `send_initial_heartbeat(node, pub, target)` (Milestone 24 above later added the `pub->reliable` gate this needed) called right next to the existing `deliver_durability_backlog()` call in `decode_update_entities()`'s own `tt_KIND_TOPIC_SUBSCRIBER` branch, same trigger, same target. **New tests** (`tests/test_heartbeat.c`, +4): a newly-discovered peer for a reliable Publisher gets an immediate unicast Heartbeat with the correct `[first_available_seq_no, last_seq_no]` range; a best-effort Publisher discovering a new peer sends none; an already-known peer's periodic UPDATE refresh (deduped before `upsert_peer()` ever re-runs) doesn't re-trigger one; a Publisher with both RELIABLE and DURABLE set sends both the backlog *and* the initial Heartbeat to a newly-discovered peer. `make test`/`make sanitize`/`platform/freertos`/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` all pass. **Real HIL, two separate experiments, both negative**: (1) the always-on discovery-triggered Heartbeat itself, exercised automatically by the existing `loss1/5/10_reliable` scenario (no flag needed) - `loss_pct` stayed flat at **0.1% / 0.1% / 0.1%** across 1%/5%/10% tc loss, unchanged from before this milestone. (2) A temporary `-H <seconds>` flag (`TT_EXAMPLE_OPT_HEARTBEAT`, `examples/linux/common/cli_opts.h`/`.c`) plus a `loss${pct}_reliable_heartbeat` scenario (`-R -H 0.05`, `.github/scripts/run_perf.sh`) tested Milestone 22's own periodic Heartbeat layered on top, mirroring the reverted `-D` (DURABLE) experiment's own precedent exactly - also **0.1% / 0.1% / 0.1%**, byte-identical to plain `reliable`, at all three levels. Both confirm the reasoning above precisely: neither Heartbeat flavor can rescue samples already evicted from a cache too shallow to survive as long as discovery/loss recovery actually takes (a hypothesis Milestone 24 above went on to test more directly by widening the cache itself instead - also negative). **Reverted** (commits reverting the `-H` wiring's own two commits, matching the `-D` experiment's own "tried and answered, reverted" precedent): the CLI flag, the temporary scenario, and its one-off job-summary table - kept the core discovery-triggered Heartbeat feature itself (a real, permanent capability, unlike the negative-result experiment layered on top of it to test periodic Heartbeat specifically). | 18, 20, 22 | ✅ done |

| **24** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #4/#5 follow-up: unify RELIABILITY/DURABILITY into one cache, raise depth to 64** - real DDS/RTPS doesn't keep a separately-sized cache for DURABILITY at all: at the TRANSIENT_LOCAL level this package implements, a late-joining reader just gets whatever's currently in the Writer's own single History Cache, the same one `HISTORY.depth`/`RESOURCE_LIMITS` already size for RELIABILITY's own retransmission - there's no independent "durability depth" concept to keep in sync with anything, because there's only ever one cache. TickLE used to keep two (`struct tt_ReliableCache`/`struct tt_DurableCache`, Milestone 19), each with its own depth constant and a `_Static_assert` (Milestone 20) to keep them from drifting apart, doubling storage for a Publisher wanting both. **Unified**: `struct tt_DurableCache`/`tt_DurableCacheEntry`/`tt_MAX_DURABLE_HISTORY`/the sync `_Static_assert` all removed; `tt_Publisher.durable_cache` replaced with `bool durable` (VOLATILE/TRANSIENT_LOCAL, gating `deliver_durability_backlog()`) reading off the *same* `reliable_cache` RELIABILITY already uses - either, both, or neither may still be requested independently. **A real coupling bug found while doing this**: Milestone 23's own discovery-triggered Heartbeat (below) gated purely on `reliable_cache != NULL` - fine when that pointer doubled as "opted into RELIABLE," but once DURABILITY-only also needed the same non-NULL pointer for its own storage, a durable-but-not-reliable Publisher started silently emitting Heartbeats nobody asked for. Fixed with a new explicit `bool reliable` field (mirroring `durable`) that `send_initial_heartbeat()` now also requires - `tt_Publisher_set_heartbeat_period()` needs no matching check, since calling it at all is already the caller's own explicit opt-in; `process_acknack()` needs no matching check either, since it only ever answers an ACKNACK a Subscriber actually sent (which itself requires the Subscriber's own independent `sub->reliable` opt-in), never unprompted. **Depth**: `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` raised 10 → 64, `tt_RELIABLE_BITMAP_BITS`'s own hard ceiling (a single ACKNACK can never name a gap wider than that, so more slots couldn't be selectively recovered from anyway) - tested specifically to check whether Milestone 18's own still-unconfirmed residual ~0.1% `loss_pct` floor (flat across every tc loss level, unmoved by durability backlog push, discovery-triggered Heartbeat, or periodic Heartbeat - see Milestone 23 below) was a retention-window problem: at the loss-injection harness's own tuned 280us send interval, depth 10 retains only ~2.8ms of real time, depth 64 retains ~17.9ms - comfortably past a plausible discovery-completion latency, if that were the cause. **`rmw_tickle` side**: `rmw_create_publisher()`'s two independent allocate-and-clamp blocks (one per policy, one per old depth cap) merged into one - `pub_impl->reliable_cache` allocated once if *either* `RMW_QOS_POLICY_RELIABILITY_RELIABLE` or `RMW_QOS_POLICY_DURABILITY_TRANSIENT_LOCAL` is requested, `.reliable`/`.durable` set to match which; `rmw_tickle_publisher_t.durable_cache` field removed, freed once instead of twice. Fixes a real pre-existing asymmetry as a side effect: ROS 2's own `rmw_qos_profile_t.depth` is a single shared field regardless (both policies always read the identical requested depth - already noted in Milestone 20's own row), so a depth between the old, smaller DURABILITY cap (8) and the old, larger RELIABILITY cap (10) used to reject a combined RELIABLE+TRANSIENT_LOCAL publisher outright even though RELIABILITY alone would have accepted it; one cap now, that gap is gone. **A second, structural consequence of depth == bitmap width found via the test suite**: `skip_unrecoverable_backlog()`'s own bulk-skip (acknack_retry()'s give-up path, Milestone 18) becomes provably a no-op once `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY == tt_RELIABLE_BITMAP_BITS` - any position `received_bitmap` can even represent (bit 0..63) is now, by construction, always within a 64-wide window, since `update_reliable_ack()`'s own oversized-gap check already catches anything wider before it's ever recorded in the bitmap at all; `tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c`'s own `test_acknack_retry_skips_unrecoverable_backlog_on_giveup` (which relied on a gap "6 past depth" still fitting in the bitmap - only possible when depth < bitmap width) rewritten as `test_acknack_retry_does_not_bulk_skip_when_depth_equals_bitmap_width`, confirming the no-op directly instead of asserting on an now-impossible scenario. **Tests**: `tests/test_durability_pubsub.c`/`tests/test_heartbeat.c` updated to set `reliable`/`durable` on the shared cache instead of a separate `durable_cache`; the combined RELIABLE+DURABLE regression test (`test_durability_backlog_recovered_via_acknack_when_reliable_too`) simplified now that the depth-sync invariant it was guarding is structural rather than asserted. `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan)/`platform/freertos` build/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` (bare invocation) all pass; `colcon build`/`colcon test` against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install - 10/10 green - plus `clang-tidy` against the real generated `compile_commands.json` for the two changed `rmw_tickle` files, clean. **Real HIL result (the actual question this depth change was for)**: `loss1/5/10_reliable`'s own `loss_pct` at depth 64 - **0.1% / 0.1% / 0.1%**, byte-identical to depth 10's own result, across all three tc loss levels. **The retention-window hypothesis is now disproven, not just unconfirmed** - a 6x deeper cache (2.8ms → 17.9ms) made no measurable difference at all, ruling out "the cache is evicted before discovery completes" as Milestone 18's own residual floor's cause. Combined with Milestone 23's own two negative Heartbeat results below, every mechanism this investigation has tried that operates on *retained samples* (deeper cache, backlog push, either Heartbeat flavor) has now failed to move this floor - strong evidence the real cause is somewhere *before* a sample is ever retained at all (e.g. a silent early drop - `process_data()`'s own `find_endpoint() == NULL` early return predates every mechanism tried so far and was never re-isolated, per Milestone 20's own still-open note below) rather than anything about how long or how proactively retained samples are made available. Depth kept at 64 (harmless on real HIL, no observed downside, and the max this build supports regardless) rather than reverted, unlike the negative-result experiments below - the cache unification itself is worth keeping independent of the depth question, and 64 costs nothing further to leave in place. | 18, 19, 20, 22 | ✅ done |

| **25** | ✅ **The actual root cause of Milestone 18's own residual RELIABLE `loss_pct` floor, found and fixed**: not a TickLE-core bug at all - `examples/linux/perf/perf_server.c`'s own `track_arrival()` (the measurement tool itself) was massively over-counting false drops. A ground-truth `seen_seq[]` diagnostic (temporary, `src/tickle.c`'s own equally-temporary `find_endpoint() == NULL` diagnostic from the same investigation pass came back negative and was already removed - see the paragraph above) recorded every seq_no ever actually delivered, independent of `track_arrival()`'s own windowed bookkeeping, and cross-checked it against `total_dropped` at the end of each run: for `loss1_reliable`/`loss5_reliable`, `track_arrival()` reported 32/38 dropped while a direct scan found **0** genuinely missing - **100% false positives**. **Root cause**: `track_arrival()`'s exact-match (`delta == 0`) branch shifts `pending_bitmap` *before* checking bit0 for the next absorb - copied from `tt_Subscriber`'s own `advance_ack_seq_no()` (`tickle.c`), which is correct *there* under its own `+j` bit convention (bit0 pre-shift means the position that just arrived, already handled by the increment, safe to discard) - but this file's own bitmap deliberately uses a *different* `+1+j` convention (its own pre-existing comment explains why: no wire-format constraint to match, unlike `tt_AckNackHeader.bitmap`), under which bit0 pre-shift instead means "was the position `expected_seq` was just advanced *to* already received out of order" - exactly the question the next iteration needs answered, and shifting first discards it unread. Every time a recovered gap's own immediate successor had already arrived out of order (an unremarkable, frequent shape under real RELIABLE recovery - the very thing `GAP_WINDOW_BITS` exists to tolerate), that position got stuck "unconfirmed" forever and was later miscounted as a genuine drop by the overflow branch or `finalize_gap_tracking()`. **Fix**: check bit0 before shifting instead (`track_arrival()`'s own updated comment explains the corrected ordering in full). **Verified two ways**: (1) a standalone simulation (`/tmp` scratch, not committed - 2000 trials, 0% real loss injected, ~5% of arrivals reordered by up to 20 positions, the realistic RELIABLE-recovery shape) - the old ordering reported a false drop on **0/2000** trials matching ground truth (100% false-positive rate); the fix matched ground truth on 1984/2000, the remaining 16 explained by the synthetic shuffle's own compounding swaps occasionally exceeding `GAP_WINDOW_BITS` (1024) outright - a real, documented, unrelated limitation (`track_arrival()`'s own overflow branch), not a remaining bug. (2) Real HIL, the actual bar this whole investigation has used throughout: `loss1_reliable`/`loss5_reliable`'s own `loss_pct` - **0.1% → 0.0%, exactly**, ground-truth cross-check confirms 0 reported vs 0 genuinely missing at both levels. `loss10_reliable` improved dramatically (54 → 17 reported) but not to zero - ground truth found 3 genuinely missing against 17 still reported, a real but much smaller residual false-positive rate at the highest loss level specifically, plausibly the same legitimate-overflow shape the simulation's own 16 explained mismatches demonstrate (10% tc loss produces more frequent, more clustered loss bursts, more likely to occasionally push a real recovery past the window for real) - not yet root-caused further. `make test`/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` all pass; no `src/tickle.c`/TickLE-core change needed at all - confirms, retroactively, that every negative result from Milestones 19 (durability push), 22/23 (both Heartbeat flavors), and 24 (depth 64) was correct as far as it went: none of them could have fixed a measurement-tool bug in a completely different file. **Not yet done**: the `loss10_reliable`-specific small residual (17 vs 3) - the ground-truth `seen_seq[]`/DIAG diagnostic (`src/…` — see `examples/linux/perf/perf_server.c`'s own doc comments) is deliberately left in place (not yet removed, unlike this session's other TEMPORARY diagnostics) specifically to keep investigating this one remaining gap; a first concrete next step would be raising `GAP_WINDOW_BITS` further (currently 1024) and re-running `loss10_reliable` specifically to see whether that alone closes it, mirroring this same milestone's own "wider window, does it help" methodology. | 18, 20, 23, 24 | ✅ done |

| **26** | ✅ **Loss-injection harness re-tuned for real, visible differentiation post-Milestone-25**: with `track_arrival()`'s own false-positive bug fixed, `run_perf.sh`'s own `loss1/5/10_reliable` scenarios needed re-tuning from a clean slate - every prior depth/interval calibration in this file's own history was measured through that same bug. Two runtime knobs, not rebuilds: `tt_MAX_RELIABLE_HISTORY` stays at 64 (`tt_RELIABLE_BITMAP_BITS`'s own hard ceiling - lowering a build-time constant to tune one HIL experiment would cap every caller, not just this one), and a new `-K <depth>` flag (`examples/linux/perf/perf_client.c`, `cli_opts.h`/`.c`) exposes `struct tt_ReliableCache.depth` - already a real, independently-configurable runtime field - exactly mirroring DDS's own `RESOURCE_LIMITS` (build-time ceiling) vs. `HISTORY.depth` (runtime request) split. Real HIL search, `RELIABLE_CACHE_DEPTH` at the existing 280us `LOSS_TEST_INTERVAL_SEC`: depth 64 → ground truth 0/0/3 genuinely-lost samples at 1%/5%/10% (too close to zero to see); 16 → 0/1/6 (real differentiation, still rounds away to the job summary's own displayed "0.0" at one decimal); 10 → 0/0/5; 8 → 0/2/5 - all three narrower depths landed in the same "real but sub-display-threshold" band (~need 18+ out of ~35,714, ~0.05%, to round up to a visible "0.1"). Depth alone couldn't clear that threshold at this interval, so the *other* half of the eviction-window product (depth * interval) got retuned instead, keeping depth at 8 (the bottom of the requested range): `LOSS_TEST_INTERVAL_SEC` narrowed 280us → 140us, halving the real-time window depth=8 gives a recovery round trip to complete without eviction. Real HIL result: **`loss10_reliable` loss_pct 0.0 → 0.3, visibly nonzero** (ground truth: 123 genuinely-lost samples out of ~66,469 at this faster rate), while `loss1_reliable`/`loss5_reliable` stayed at 0.0 (ground truth: 1 and 8 genuinely-lost samples respectively) - the target shape (10% shows real, visible loss; 1%/5% stay clean) achieved. `make test`/`sanitize`/`platform/freertos`/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` all pass on every step; `tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c`'s own depth-vs-bitmap-width regression test parametrized (Milestone 25's own row) so it never needs another rewrite as this tuning continues to evolve. The `seen_seq[]`/DIAG ground-truth diagnostic (Milestone 25) stayed in place throughout this whole search and was what made each iteration's real result legible - still not removed, now serving double duty confirming this milestone's own tuning as well as Milestone 25's still-open `loss10_reliable` residual (see that row's own "Not yet done"). | 24, 25 | ✅ done |

| **27** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #3 (LIVELINESS) follow-up: `not_alive_count` given real data** - from a separate "TickLE Plan" session's own DDS-parity review (found by reading TickLE core's discovery/matching code directly, not a test failure): `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_CHANGED.not_alive_count` (a live snapshot per the real `rmw_liveliness_changed_status_t` spec - "currently not alive," not a delta) was always reported as 0, because `tt_Discovery` never kept a tombstone for a peer `check_liveliness()` (`tickle.c`) presumed dead - it was just removed outright, the same as a normal departure, leaving no "known but currently dead" set to report a live count from. **Fix**: `struct tt_DiscoveredEntity` (`tickle.h`) gains a `bool alive` field. A liveliness *timeout* now tombstones (`alive = false`, entity otherwise kept intact - name/type/kind all still there) via a new `tombstone_discovered_entities_from_source()`, instead of freeing the slot the way an explicit farewell or a dropped-from-a-fresh-announce departure still does (`forget_discovered_entities_from_source()`, unchanged) - matching the real RMW spec's own explicit distinction ("becomes not alive... for some reason *other than* the normal deletion of that Publisher"). `tt_Discovery_count()` only counts alive entries (matching its own "topic list" introspection use - `ros2 topic list` shouldn't show a dead node's own topic); `tt_Discovery_find()` still returns a tombstoned entity (with `alive == false`) rather than `NULL`, so a caller can tell "never seen" apart from "seen, now presumed dead." `upsert_discovered_entity()`'s own slot search falls back to reclaiming the first tombstoned slot when no truly-empty one is left, so a table saturated with old tombstones can't starve a genuinely new entity out of a slot it would otherwise have room for. **`rmw_tickle` side**: `rmw_graph.c` gains `count_not_alive_matching_locked()` alongside the existing `count_matching_locked()` (which now excludes tombstones from its own count, an implicit correctness fix - a presumed-dead Publisher was previously still counted as "matched"), exposed as `rmw_tickle_count_not_alive_matching_locked()` (`rmw_tickle.h`). `check_subscription_liveliness()` (`rmw_subscription.c`) tracks the new live `not_alive_count` snapshot in a new `rmw_tickle_liveliness_changed_status_t.not_alive_count` field, alongside the *pre-existing*, independent alive-count-delta-based `not_alive.total_count`/`unread_count` tracking (kept as-is - that already correctly answers "how many *went* not-alive," a different question from "how many *are* not-alive right now"). `rmw_event.c`'s own `rmw_take_event()` reads the real value instead of the old hardcoded `0`. **New `tests/test_discovery.c` coverage** (2 new cases): a liveliness timeout tombstones rather than frees (`tt_Discovery_count()` drops it, `tt_Discovery_find()` still returns it with `alive == false`, all other fields intact), and a later re-announce from the same source correctly flips it back to alive (reusing `process_update()`'s own pre-existing forget-then-readd cycle, no special-case code needed there); a full table of nothing but tombstones still makes room for a genuinely new entity from a different source, reclaiming the first tombstoned slot rather than logging "table full" and dropping it. `make test`/`sanitize`/`platform/freertos`/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` all pass; `colcon build`/`colcon test` against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install - 10/10 green - plus `clang-tidy` against the real generated `compile_commands.json` for every changed `rmw_tickle` file, clean. **Not done here** (a separate, harder gap the same peer review flagged, still open): `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_LOST` - see its own existing note in Milestone 21's own row; TickLE's only local "am I still signaling liveliness" mechanism is the poll thread's own periodic self-announce, so a self-check from that same execution context is structurally unfalsifiable (if the check runs, the announce already did too) - needs a genuinely independent observer of actual publish activity vs. the announce timer, which TickLE's single-poll-thread model doesn't have an obvious source for yet; worth a design pass before an implementation attempt, not a small follow-on to this milestone. | 21 | ✅ done |

| **28** | 🔶 **Design pass only, not yet implemented - two structural QoS gaps from "TickLE Plan"'s own DDS-parity review** (found by reading TickLE core's discovery/matching code directly against real DDS semantics, not from a test failure): (a) QoS-based RxO (Request-vs-Offered) matching doesn't exist anywhere in TickLE's discovery/topic-matching path - a BEST_EFFORT Publisher and a RELIABLE Subscriber on the same topic today silently "match" instead of DDS's own "cleanly never connects, visibly"; (b) `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_LOST` is structurally, permanently `{0, 0}` (Milestone 21's own already-documented limit) - the only local liveliness signal and any self-check of it run from the same execution context, making a failure structurally unfalsifiable from inside. Both large enough that the review itself suggested a design-review milestone before any implementation, rather than diving straight into code the way most of this roadmap has - see the two standalone paragraphs immediately below this row for the actual design sketches. | 15, 21, 27 | 🔶 design only, not implemented |

**(a) RxO matching, design sketch**: `decode_update_entities()`/`upsert_peer()` (`tickle.c`) connect a Publisher and Subscriber purely by `tt_hash_id(topic_name, endpoint_name)` - reliability/durability are per-endpoint *local* switches (`tt_Publisher.reliable`/`.durable`, `tt_Subscriber.reliable`), never negotiated or even compared. Concretely: an incompatible pair still gets a peer-list entry, unicast/backlog/Heartbeat all proceed, and the Subscriber's own ACKNACK just goes unanswered forever (`process_acknack()` finds `pub->reliable_cache == NULL` and silently returns) - degraded, but silently. `rmw_qos_profile_check_compatible()` (Milestone 15) already has the real compatibility logic - it's just a plain public function nothing calls automatically; real DDS enforces this at the discovery protocol level, which needs QoS riding on the wire first (today's `struct tt_UpdateEntity`, `tickle.h`, carries only `endpoint_id`/`kind`, no QoS at all). Sketch, not committed to: (1) wire format - `tt_UpdateEntity` (or a QoS sub-block after it) gains at minimum a `reliable`/`durable` bit pair per entity, matching what TickLE core actually implements today (a real `tt_VERSION` wire-protocol change). (2) Where the gate lives - `decode_update_entities()`'s own `tt_KIND_TOPIC_SUBSCRIBER` branch (Publisher side) would need to skip `upsert_peer()`/`deliver_durability_backlog()`/`send_initial_heartbeat()` entirely for an incompatible remote Subscriber's own announce. (3) **A real, TickLE-specific limitation this design has to be honest about**: TickLE broadcasts DATA to everyone on the segment by default (peer-list unicast is only an *optimization* for small known-peer counts, not how delivery is gated) - refusing to `upsert_peer()` only blocks the *enhancements*, it can't stop the raw broadcast DATA from physically reaching and being processed by an "incompatible" Subscriber's own `process_data()`, unlike real DDS/RTPS where reader-writer matching gates delivery at the transport level itself. True symmetric blocking would need the *Subscriber* side to also track each discovered Publisher's own offered QoS (a new lightweight cache, plausibly reusing the existing `struct tt_DiscoveredEntity`-style mechanism) and gate `process_data()`'s own delivery-to-application step on it too - real additional scope beyond the Publisher-side half. (4) Surfacing this at the `rmw_tickle` layer - `RMW_EVENT_OFFERED_INCOMPATIBLE_QOS`/`REQUESTED_INCOMPATIBLE_QOS` don't exist in this codebase's own event handling at all yet (`rmw_event.c` only handles the QoS roadmap #2/#3 events so far) - would need their own status-tracking struct, matching the `rmw_tickle_event_status_t` pattern every other event here already uses. Given the size (a real wire-protocol *and* discovery-state-machine change, likely two-sided), the review's own suggestion stands: split into this design milestone (done) and a separate implementation milestone whenever picked up, not bundled into one pass.

**(b) `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_LOST`, design sketch**: the only local "am I still signaling liveliness" mechanism is the poll thread's own periodic self-announce (`node_update()`, `tickle.c`), driven by the *same* scheduler loop (`tt_Node_poll()`) any self-check would also have to run from - if the check executes at all, the announce already fired too, making a failure structurally unfalsifiable from inside that same execution context. Real use case this can't serve: detecting the local process's own hang (a stalled callback, or the whole poll loop wedged) - something a genuinely *independent* observer could catch but a same-thread self-check never can. The fix needs an observer that runs outside `tt_Node_poll()`'s own execution entirely - not a TickLE-core change (TickLE core has no threading model of its own; `poll_thread` is `rmw_tickle`'s own pthread, `rmw_node.c`), but an `rmw_tickle`-layer one. Sketch: a lightweight watchdog thread per node (spun up alongside `poll_thread` itself, `rmw_node.c`'s own `rmw_create_node()`) that does nothing but periodically check a shared, atomically-updated "last time `tt_Node_poll()` actually returned" timestamp (`poll_thread`'s own loop already has this timing information for free, just needs to publish it somewhere the watchdog can read) against a deadline - if stale, mark every live Publisher on that node `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_LOST`. Architecturally consistent with `rmw_tickle`'s own existing single-poll-thread-per-node model (one more thread, not a redesign of it), and gives a genuinely independent signal a same-thread check structurally cannot. Trade-offs not yet weighed: a second thread per node is a real resource/complexity cost this package has avoided everywhere else; whether the watchdog's own check interval should be configurable or fixed; and whether this is worth building at all given DDS implementations with a similarly constrained single-participant-thread model have the identical limitation in practice. Not committed to an implementation - flagged as the leading design direction, nothing more.

**Not addressed by this design pass** (lower priority per the same review): `LIVELINESS` staying `AUTOMATIC`-only (`MANUAL_BY_TOPIC`/`_BY_NODE` rejected outright, no `rmw_publisher_assert_liveliness()`-style explicit assertion API) - real-world use of manual liveliness assertion in ROS 2 is rare, tracked as an open roadmap row (see #3's own table entry above), not a design priority right now.

| **29** | ✅ **QoS roadmap #6 (LIFESPAN)** - "TickLE Plan"'s own DDS-parity review resequenced this to lowest priority behind the four rows above, but it turned out not to need a design pass of its own: unlike RxO matching/`LIVELINESS_LOST` (Milestone 28), it needed no wire-protocol change at all, since `tt_DataHeader.timestamp` (the sample's own write-time, `tt_Publisher_publish()`) was already on the wire regardless of any QoS - every Subscriber already receives it whether or not either side requested LIFESPAN. Two independent, uncoordinated expiry points, matching real DDS's "as if it had never been sent" wording exactly: (a) **Publisher-side** (TickLE core, `tickle.c`) - a new `tt_ReliableCacheEntry.timestamp` (set in `cache_reliable_sample()`) and `tt_Publisher.lifespan_duration_ns` (0 = disabled, plain caller-owned field, same convention as `reliable`/`durable`) feed a small `reliable_cache_entry_expired()` helper that `deliver_durability_backlog()` and a new `find_resendable_cache_entry()` (split out of `process_acknack()` to keep its own cognitive complexity under clang-tidy's threshold) both consult - an expired entry is skipped for backlog delivery and silently not retransmitted on NACK, even though the bytes are still physically cached (KEEP_LAST eviction is unaffected, a separate mechanism). (b) **Subscription-side** (`rmw_tickle`, `rmw_subscription.c`) - a new `rmw_tickle_subscriber_t.lifespan_ns` (from `qos_profile->lifespan`) makes `rmw_take_with_info()` drop any already-stale entries off the front of its own queue (comparing against `source_timestamp`, which already came from that same wire timestamp) before returning the real head, "as if it had never been sent" the same way. `rmw_tickle_validate_qos_profile()` (`rmw_qos.c`) now accepts any finite `lifespan` - same "any finite value, purely local" reasoning DEADLINE (Milestone 21) already established - fixing `test_qos.c`'s own now-inverted assertion at the same time. Verified with two new whitebox TickLE-core tests (`tests/test_reliable_pubsub.c`'s `test_process_acknack_skips_expired_sample`, `tests/test_durability_pubsub.c`'s `test_durability_skips_expired_backlog_entries` - both drive `test_mock_now` past the configured lifespan and assert the expected no-op) plus the full existing suite (`make test`/`make sanitize`/`platform/freertos` build/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy`, all clean) and a real `colcon build`/`colcon test` rebuild of `rmw_tickle` (all 10 tests, including the corrected `test_qos`, passing). | 7, 19, 20, 21, 24 | ✅ done |

| **30** | ✅ **`RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_LOST` watchdog thread** - implements Milestone 28(b)'s own design sketch essentially as written, after "TickLE Plan" confirmed that design pass needed no further review: a genuinely independent thread (`rmw_node.c`), not a same-thread self-check, is the only way to detect `poll_thread` itself hanging - a same-thread check can never run at all if the thing it's checking is what's wedged. New `rmw_tickle_node_t.poll_thread_last_return_ns` (`atomic_uint_least64_t`): `poll_thread_main()` stores `tt_get_ns()` here every time `tt_Node_poll()` actually returns, initialized once up front (before either thread starts) so a freshly-created node's own zeroed default can't look like an instant hang. New `watchdog_thread_main()`, started right after `poll_thread` in `rmw_create_node()` (non-fatal if it fails to start - LIVELINESS_LOST just stays permanently un-fireable for that node, same "degrade, don't fail node creation" precedent DEADLINE's own scheduling-failure path already set) and joined in `rmw_destroy_node()` (after `poll_thread`'s own join, before `tt_Node_destroy()`/`pthread_mutex_destroy()` - no race, since the join can't return until the watchdog has fully exited its loop). Checks every `RMW_TICKLE_WATCHDOG_CHECK_INTERVAL_NS` (`tt_NODE_UPDATE_INTERVAL`, reusing this node's own existing self-announce cadence rather than a third arbitrary number) whether `poll_thread_last_return_ns` has gone stale past `RMW_TICKLE_WATCHDOG_STALE_THRESHOLD_NS` (`tt_LIVELINESS_MISS_THRESHOLD * tt_NODE_UPDATE_INTERVAL`, the exact same floor `rmw_qos.c`'s own `liveliness_lease_duration` acceptance check already established as "TickLE core's own fastest possible peer-death detection latency") - both `#define`s local to `rmw_node.c`, no new public config surface needed. Edge-triggered (an `already_lost` latch): a sustained hang bumps every live Publisher's `liveliness_lost` exactly once (`mark_liveliness_lost()`, new - takes `node->mutex` itself since it isn't running on the poll thread, then recovers each `rmw_tickle_publisher_t*` from `tickle_node.endpoints[]` via the same `offsetof()` trick `subscriber_callback()` already uses, `struct tt_Endpoint` being each `tt_Publisher`'s own first member), matching real DDS's "lease expired" wording (an event, not a continuously-repeating one) rather than firing again every single check for as long as the hang lasts; recovering below the threshold re-arms it for a later, separate hang. Shutdown stays responsive without a `tt_Node_interrupt()`-equivalent: the watchdog slices its own sleep into `RMW_TICKLE_WATCHDOG_SHUTDOWN_POLL_NS` (50ms) chunks and re-checks `watchdog_thread_running` between them, rather than blocking a full check interval at a time. **Updated stale claims**: `rmw_tickle_publisher_t.liveliness_lost`'s own doc comment, `test_events.c`'s own LIVELINESS_LOST smoke-test comment, and this file's Milestone 21/27/28 rows all previously called this "structurally, permanently `{0, 0}`" - now real, though a perfectly healthy node (the case every *other* existing test exercises) still correctly reports `{0, 0}`, since its `poll_thread` never actually stalls. **New `test/test_liveliness_lost_watchdog.c`** (registered in `CMakeLists.txt` alongside the other black-box `rmw_tickle` tests): unlike `test_events.c`'s own event-plumbing smoke tests, proving a real hang is detected needs an actual stall, not a mocked clock (`rmw_tickle`'s own test suite has no `tt_get_ns()` mock the way TickLE core's `tests/test_mock.h` does) - reaches into the public `rmw_tickle_c/rmw_tickle.h` header directly (a legitimate public struct, not a private implementation detail) to hold `node_impl->mutex` from the test's own thread for `tt_LIVELINESS_MISS_THRESHOLD * tt_NODE_UPDATE_INTERVAL + 1s` (recomputed from those same two public `tickle/config.h` constants rather than hardcoding "3 seconds" and silently drifting from `rmw_node.c`'s own actual threshold if either ever changes) - the same mutex `poll_thread_main()` needs to reacquire before its next `tt_Node_poll()` call, so this is a real, if externally-induced, stall of `poll_thread`'s own progress. Confirms: a healthy node reports no spurious loss (`rmw_wait()` correctly times out, `RMW_RET_TIMEOUT`, not `RMW_RET_OK`, on a short wait); after the induced stall, `rmw_wait()` (a real 2-second wait, giving the watchdog's own blocked-on-the-same-mutex `mark_liveliness_lost()` call room to run and broadcast once unblocked, rather than racing a fixed short sleep against it) reports the event ready, and `rmw_take_event()` returns `total_count >= 1`. Verified: `colcon build --packages-select rmw_tickle` clean; `colcon test` - 6/6 green (12 tests, the new one taking the suite from ~0.7s to ~4.8s total, the added real stall's own cost); `clang-tidy`/`clang-format` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed/new file (one real finding, fixed: the new test's own two magic-number literals, extracted into named constants matching `test_events.c`'s own `NS_PER_MS` precedent). | 21, 27, 28 | ✅ done |

| **31** | ✅ **RxO (Request-vs-Offered) QoS matching for RELIABILITY/DURABILITY** - implements Milestone 28(a)'s own design sketch, scoped to the two policies TickLE's own wire protocol can actually carry a boolean opinion about today (per that sketch's own "at minimum a reliable/durable bit pair" framing) - `LIVELINESS`/`DEADLINE` compatibility (numeric lease/duration comparison, per real `rmw_qos_profile_check_compatible()`) would need transmitting actual durations on the wire, real additional scope not attempted here, tracked as an open follow-on below. **Wire change** (`tickle.h`): `tt_VERSION` bumped `1` -> `2` - the first bump this constant has ever needed - backing a new `qos` byte on `struct tt_UpdateEntity` (two bits, `tt_UPDATE_QOS_RELIABLE`/`_DURABLE`; always `0` for services/clients, which have no wire-visible QoS negotiation at all here). Safe without any version-straddling parsing: `process_packet()` already rejects `header->version < tt_VERSION` outright, so a sender must already be on this same wire layout by the time `decode_update_entities()` ever reads the new field. Same bit positions mean two things depending on which entity kind carries them - *offered* on a `TOPIC_PUBLISHER` entity (`tt_Publisher.reliable`/`.durable`, unchanged), *requested* on a `TOPIC_SUBSCRIBER` one (`tt_Subscriber.reliable`, unchanged, and a genuinely new `tt_Subscriber.durable` - Subscribers never had *any* way to express "I require TRANSIENT_LOCAL" before this milestone, since backlog delivery was always a purely Publisher-side decision with no reader opt-out concept). New shared `endpoint_qos_bits()` (`tickle.c`, mirrors `endpoint_type_name()`'s own kind-`switch` shape exactly) computes it once, for both `encode_update_entities()` (announcing) and (indirectly, via the same bits already decoded) the two gates below. **Two independent gates**, not one - Milestone 28(a)'s own design pass already flagged why a Publisher-side gate alone wouldn't be enough: TickLE broadcasts DATA by default, and peer-list unicast is only ever an *optimization* for a small known-peer count, not how delivery itself is gated, so refusing to `upsert_peer()` an incompatible remote Subscriber blocks only the *enhancements* (durability backlog, discovery-triggered Heartbeat, unicast), never the raw broadcast DATA a BEST_EFFORT Publisher already reaches every listener with regardless. (1) **Publisher-side** (`decode_update_entities()`'s own `tt_KIND_TOPIC_SUBSCRIBER` branch): an incompatible remote Subscriber's own `upsert_peer()`/`deliver_durability_backlog()`/`send_initial_heartbeat()` are all skipped together - real, but the lesser-consequence half. (2) **Subscriber-side** (new `subscriber_incompatible_with_publisher()`, called from `process_data()` before `update_reliable_ack()` or delivery to `callback`) - the actually consequential half, since it's what stops broadcast DATA delivery outright: looks up the sending Publisher's own last-announced `qos` via the *existing* discovery table (new `struct tt_DiscoveredEntity.qos`, populated by `upsert_discovered_entity()` the same way `type`/`name` already are - reusing this table rather than inventing a second cache was Milestone 28(a)'s own suggested direction, and it turned out to fit directly: real DDS's own equivalent, the "Publications" built-in topic, is exactly this same kind of discovery-table row). Fails **open** (delivers) whenever there's nothing to check against: no discovery cache attached at all (a raw TickLE-core caller that never called `tt_Node_set_discovery()` sees zero behavior change from this milestone), or this Publisher hasn't been discovered yet (DATA arriving before its own first UPDATE - a narrow startup race, not a real incompatibility; giving the benefit of the doubt here beats dropping a legitimately compatible pair's very first samples). **`rmw_tickle` wiring** (`rmw_subscription.c`): `rmw_create_subscription()` now also sets the new `tickle_subscriber.durable` from `RMW_QOS_POLICY_DURABILITY_TRANSIENT_LOCAL == qos_profile->durability` - without this one line, every real ROS 2 Subscription would keep it at its `zero_allocate()` default (`false`) regardless of what it actually requested, and the new DURABILITY gate could never fire for `rmw_tickle`'s own actual users (the Publisher side of this same wiring, `pub_impl->tickle_publisher.durable`, already existed since Milestone 24 - only the Subscriber's own *requested* half was new). **New `tests/test_rxo_matching.c`** (7 cases, whitebox, same `#include "../src/tickle.c"` approach as `test_reliable_pubsub.c`/`test_durability_pubsub.c`): both gates' compatible and incompatible cases (RELIABLE-vs-BEST_EFFORT, TRANSIENT_LOCAL-vs-VOLATILE), plus the two fail-open cases (no discovery attached; Publisher not yet discovered) - `test_publisher_side_gate_*` checks `count_peers(pub.peers)` directly rather than inferring it from `test_mock_send_to_call_count`, since a BEST_EFFORT/VOLATILE Publisher's own gate gets exercised with `reliable_cache == NULL` (nothing to back a backlog/Heartbeat with even if the gate let it through) - the peer-list itself is the one observable side effect that isolates what this gate specifically controls. Existing `tests/test_malformed_packets.c`'s own `test_rejects_old_version` needed no change at all - it already computes its "too old" version as `tt_VERSION - 1`, not a hardcoded literal, so the bump adjusted it automatically. Verified: `make test`/`sanitize`/`platform/freertos`/`clang-format`/`clang-tidy` all pass (TickLE core); `colcon build`/`colcon test` against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install - 12/12 green, unaffected by the version bump since both processes in every test share one just-built binary; `clang-tidy`/`clang-format` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed `rmw_tickle` file; `DESIGN.md`'s own `tt_UpdateEntity` class diagram updated to list the new `qos` field. **Explicitly deferred, not attempted here** (real, open follow-on gaps): `RMW_EVENT_OFFERED_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE`/`REQUESTED_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE` - Milestone 28(a)'s own design sketch item (4) - an incompatible pair is now silently, correctly never-connected (matching real DDS's own *behavior*), but `rclcpp` still has no way to *observe* that it happened the way it could for a real incompatible match elsewhere; would need its own status-tracking struct, matching every other `rmw_tickle_event_status_t`-backed event here (**done, Milestone 48**). `LIVELINESS`/`DEADLINE` RxO (numeric duration comparison) - needs the wire to carry actual durations, not just booleans, a bigger follow-on change than this milestone's own two-bit extension. | 15, 19, 20, 24, 28 | ✅ done |
| **48** | ✅ **`RMW_EVENT_OFFERED_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE`/`RMW_EVENT_REQUESTED_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE` observability** - Milestone 31/28(a)'s own explicitly-deferred item (4), implemented at the user's own direct request ("이대로 진행해줘", 2026-09-20) as part of a 4-item follow-up batch (A→B→4→5) TickLE Plan relayed after Milestone 47 landed. Real DDS's `rmw_qos_incompatible_event_status_t` (`total_count`/`total_count_change`/`last_policy_kind`) - no wire-level trigger exists for this in TickLE's own protocol (Milestone 31's own two gates just silently skip a mismatched pair), so this is a **periodic re-scan of the existing discovery table** instead, the same pattern `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_CHANGED`'s own `check_subscription_liveliness()` already established for an analogous "no wire trigger" event: `check_publisher_qos_incompatible()`/`check_subscription_qos_incompatible()` (`rmw_publisher.c`/`rmw_subscription.c`, both new, `tt_Node_schedule()`'d once per `RMW_TICKLE_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE_CHECK_PERIOD_NS` == `tt_NODE_UPDATE_INTERVAL`) count how many currently-alive discovered remote entities of the opposite kind on the same topic are incompatible (new `rmw_tickle_count_incompatible_subscribers_locked()`/`_publishers_locked()`, `rmw_graph.c` - `struct tt_DiscoveredEntity.qos`, already populated by Milestone 31, compared against the local entity's own `reliable`/`durable` via a shared `qos_incompatible()` helper mirroring `tickle.c`'s own `subscriber_incompatible_with_publisher()` exactly), then delta-track that count against the previous reading (new `rmw_tickle_qos_incompatible_status_t.last_incompatible_count`, mirrors `rmw_tickle_liveliness_changed_status_t.last_alive_count`) to bump `total_count`/`unread_count` only on a genuine increase - matching real DDS's own cumulative "count of discoveries," not a live snapshot. `last_policy_kind` reports `RMW_QOS_POLICY_RELIABILITY` or `_DURABILITY` (RELIABILITY checked first when both mismatch - real DDS's own wording only promises "one of the policies," not a specific one) - Milestone 49 below extends this same function to also report `_DEADLINE`/`_LIVELINESS`. Lazy, idempotent start (new `offered_qos_incompatible_monitoring_started`/`requested_qos_incompatible_monitoring_started` bools on `rmw_tickle_publisher_t`/`_subscriber_t`) - same pattern `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_CHANGED`'s own `liveliness_lease_ns == 0` gate already uses, just a plain bool here since there's no real QoS-provided duration for a `0` sentinel to double as. `rmw_event.c`/`rmw_wait_set.c` wired identically to every other `rmw_tickle_event_status_t`-backed event (`rmw_event_type_is_supported()`, `rmw_take_event()`, `event_status_for()`). **Tests**: `test_events.c` extended past its own prior LIVELINESS_CHANGED-style "smoke test only" limit (this rmw's real one-node-per-process constraint, its own module doc comment) - reaching into the public `rmw_tickle_c/rmw_tickle.h` header to inject one synthetic `struct tt_DiscoveredEntity` directly into `context_impl->discovery.entities[]` under `node_mutex` (the same technique Milestone 30's own `test_liveliness_lost_watchdog.c` already established for an equivalent reason: proving something no black-box rmw API call alone can induce) proves a **real** detected transition for both event directions, not just wiring - `rmw_wait()` genuinely blocks until the real periodic check (backed by the actual `poll_thread`, not a mocked clock) notices the injected mismatch and broadcasts, confirmed by the test's own runtime moving from ~0.2s to ~2.2s. Verified: `colcon build`/`colcon test` (10/10, `lyrical`) clean; `clang-tidy -p build/rmw_tickle`/`clang-format --dry-run --Werror` clean on every touched file (one real finding, fixed: a magic-number literal in the new test, extracted into `FAKE_REMOTE_NODE_ID`). | 28, 30, 31 | ✅ done |
| **49** | ✅ **`LIVELINESS`/`DEADLINE` RxO matching - the wire-format expansion** - Milestone 31/28(a)'s own second explicitly-deferred item, implemented right after Milestone 48 in the same user-approved 4-item batch (item "B" in TickLE Plan's own relay, flagged as needing "a real wire-format expansion" - bigger scope than Milestone 48's own pure-observability addition, confirmed true: this one touches TickLE core's own wire layout, not just `rmw_tickle`). **Real wire change** (`tickle.h`): `tt_VERSION` bumped `3` -> `4` - `struct tt_UpdateEntity` gains a new `tt_UPDATE_QOS_LIVELINESS_MANUAL` qos bit (kind: AUTOMATIC vs MANUAL_BY_TOPIC, this rmw's only two per Milestone 32) plus two real `uint64_t` fields, `deadline_duration_ns`/`liveliness_lease_duration_ns` (a bit can say "which policy," not "how long" - real DDS's own LIVELINESS policy is `(kind, lease_duration)` as one combined unit regardless, so a Subscriber may legitimately request AUTOMATIC with a tight lease requirement, not just a MANUAL_BY_TOPIC one) - `reserved[2]` pads 6 -> 8 first so both stay 4-aligned (DESIGN.md's own CDR-4 convention). `struct tt_Publisher`/`tt_Subscriber` each gain the matching `deadline_duration_ns`/`liveliness_lease_duration_ns`/`liveliness_manual` fields (offered/requested respectively) - purely wire-announcement fields TickLE core itself never enforces, mirrored into `struct tt_DiscoveredEntity` on the receiving side by `upsert_discovered_entity()` (two new params) the same way `.qos`/`.type`/`.name` already are. **The actual comparison** - new `deadline_liveliness_incompatible()` (`tickle.c`), a from-scratch re-expression of `rmw_tickle`'s own already-correct, already-tested static `rmw_qos_profile_check_compatible()` (`rmw_qos.c`) against TickLE's own plain duration/bool primitives instead of `rmw_qos_profile_t` (TickLE core has no such concept at all, being ROS/rmw-agnostic - the two can't share one implementation): DEADLINE requires offered `<=` requested (`0` = infinite/no-requirement on either side, this codebase's own universal 0-disabled convention); LIVELINESS requires `offered_manual` whenever `requested_manual`, and the lease duration itself follows the identical offered`<=`requested rule, independent of kind. Wired into **both** of Milestone 31's own existing gates - `register_subscriber_peer_on_publisher()` (Publisher-side, `struct update_peer_ctx` gains the remote Subscriber's own requested values) and `subscriber_incompatible_with_publisher()` (Subscriber-side, the consequential half) - alongside the pre-existing RELIABILITY/DURABILITY checks, not replacing them. **`rmw_tickle` wiring**: `rmw_create_publisher()`/`rmw_create_subscription()` now also populate these three new TickLE-core fields - for a Publisher, `deadline_duration_ns`/`liveliness_lease_duration_ns` are copied straight from the *already-resolved* `pub_impl->deadline_period_ns`/`.liveliness_lease_ns` (the real, enforced values Milestone 21/32 already compute, not a re-derived raw request); for a Subscription, computed **fresh** from `qos_profile` directly instead of reusing `sub_impl->liveliness_lease_ns` - that field stays lazily `0` until `RMW_EVENT_LIVELINESS_CHANGED` is actually requested (`rmw_subscription_event_init()`'s own doc comment), but RxO compatibility must reflect what QoS actually asked for regardless of which events an application happens to request. **Milestone 48's own observability extended to match**: `rmw_graph.c`'s own `qos_incompatible()`/`rmw_tickle_count_incompatible_subscribers_locked()`/`_publishers_locked()` gained the identical three-value extension (mirroring `deadline_liveliness_incompatible()`'s own logic a second time, since rmw-level code can't call TickLE-core's static function either) - `RMW_EVENT_OFFERED_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE`/`REQUESTED_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE`'s own `last_policy_kind` can now report `RMW_QOS_POLICY_DEADLINE`/`_LIVELINESS` too, not just `_RELIABILITY`/`_DURABILITY`. **Tests**: `tests/test_rxo_matching.c` gained 7 new whitebox cases (DEADLINE looser/tighter on the Subscriber-side gate, LIVELINESS-kind mismatch/match, LIVELINESS-lease mismatch on the Subscriber-side gate, plus DEADLINE and LIVELINESS-kind mismatches on the Publisher-side gate) - all passing alongside the 7 pre-existing ones; `rmw_tickle/test/test_events.c` gained one new case proving the new `offered_manual`/`requested_manual` comparison path fires a real `RMW_EVENT_OFFERED_QOS_INCOMPATIBLE` with `last_policy_kind == RMW_QOS_POLICY_LIVELINESS` (same synthetic-discovery-injection technique Milestone 48 already established), confirmed by the test's own runtime growing by another real periodic-check interval. | **50** | ✅ **`rcl_interfaces` typesupport for `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c` - unblocking a real `rclcpp::Node` over `rmw_tickle` at all** - TickLE Plan's own post-item-5 priority list, item 2, at the user's own direct request ("진행해"). **Real gap found while building item 1** (`rmw_perf_pingpong`, below, on `tickle-hil`'s own rpi pair, ROS 2 Jazzy): every `rclcpp::Node`, unconditionally, creates a `/parameter_events` (`rcl_interfaces/msg/ParameterEvent`) subscription via its own internal `NodeTimeSource` (for `use_sim_time` monitoring - confirmed via `librclcpp.so`'s own `TimeSource::NodeState::on_set_parameters` symbol and `rcl_subscription.c`'s own debug log; no `NodeOptions` flag disables it, `enable_logger_service`/`start_parameter_event_publisher` are both separate, unrelated flags). `rmw_tickle` has never had typesupport for this message at all - meaning **no real `rclcpp::Node` can be created over `rmw_tickle` on a distro with this `TimeSource` behavior**, a functional gap, not a performance one, independent of whatever application code actually runs. **Two distinct problems found and fixed, both scoped to `.msg` files only (see below for why `.srv` stays out of scope)**: (1) `rcl_interfaces`' variant-style `ParameterValue.msg` has five variable-length array fields in one struct (plus three more messages sharing a similar shape - `ParameterEvent`/`ParameterEventDescriptors`/`ListParametersResult`) - `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`'s own auto-derived capacity only supports a single trailing one (`tools/typesupport/tickle_typesupport/adapt.py`'s own documented capacity rule) - fixed with real, hand-picked `# @capacity <N>` annotations on the others (new `.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/rcl_interfaces_rmw_tickle.patch`, generated the same pristine-clone-`git diff` way every other patch in that directory already is, not hand-edited) - `1` for `ParameterValue`/`ParameterEvent` (a real "variant" type where only one of five fields is ever meaningfully populated at once, and events are individually rare) and `4`/`8` for the other two (arbitrary but real, matching this codebase's own "small hard cap, widen if a real workload needs more" convention). (2) `rcl_interfaces`' own `.srv` files (`SetParameters` etc.) all nest `Parameter`/`ParameterValue` too, but generating them hit a **separate, real bug** in `tools/typesupport/tickle_typesupport/resolve.py`'s own `Ros2Resolver._find_independent_source()`: it looks for a same-package "sibling" `.msg` relative to `os.path.dirname(input_path)` - the *currently-generating* file's own directory - which is `srv/` for a `.srv` file, not `msg/`, so `Parameter.msg`/`ParameterValue.msg` are never actually found there; the lookup falls through to a different, *unpatched* copy instead (this rig's own apt-installed `/opt/ros/jazzy` one), losing the `@capacity` annotations and reproducing the same capacity error one level deeper. **A real bug affecting any package mixing `.srv` and `.msg` with a same-package cross-reference between them, not `rcl_interfaces`-specific** - not fixed here (a core, shared-tool change deserving its own dedicated pass, not squeezed into this one under time pressure - tracked as an explicit follow-on, not silently dropped). **Scope decision, the user's own direct call after being shown both options**: rather than fix that shared-tool bug now, `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_generate_interfaces.cmake`'s own foreach loop gained a narrow, explicitly-commented skip - `rcl_interfaces`'s own `.srv` files specifically produce no `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c` output at all, while every `.msg` file (all thirteen, the full real set, not just the three `/parameter_events` itself needs) generates normally. Costs nothing not already missing: `rmw_tickle` has no RPC/service typesupport story for `rcl_interfaces` either way yet (parameter get/set services stay unavailable over `rmw_tickle`, same shape as the already-documented `TestClient`/`TestService.check_qos` conformance gaps) - and does **not** touch `rcl_interfaces`' own `rosidl_generate_interfaces()` message/service *declaration* list at all (unlike `test_msgs_rmw_tickle.patch`'s own `list(FILTER ... EXCLUDE REGEX ...)` precedent, which is safe only because `test_msgs` is exclusively a throwaway test dependency - `rcl_interfaces` is a core, widely-apt-depended-on package, so removing anything from its own struct/typesupport generation for *every* rmw would be a real, dangerous regression for anyone using it unmodified; this fix only narrows what `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c` *specifically* generates, leaving FastRTPS/CycloneDDS/introspection typesupport for every declared type completely untouched). **Verified for real, end to end, on `tickle-hil`'s own two rpis** (a genuinely provisioned `~/rmw_perf_ws`-style overlay, not a hypothetical): `rcl_interfaces` (all 13 `.msg` files, `.srv` skipped) builds clean against `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`; `rmw_perf_pingpong`'s own `ping_node`/`pong_node` (below) run over `rmw_tickle` without crashing, cross-host, both `BEST_EFFORT`/`RELIABLE`, 0% loss - the actual proof `/parameter_events` genuinely works now, not just that it compiles; `colcon build`/`colcon test` (10/10, `lyrical`) unaffected on this dev box (the CMake change is inert unless `PROJECT_NAME` is literally `rcl_interfaces`). **Explicitly not done this pass, tracked as real follow-ons**: the `Ros2Resolver` sibling-lookup bug itself (would let `.srv` support come for free once fixed); | **52** | ✅ **`Ros2Resolver`'s `.srv`-nested-`.msg` sibling-lookup bug, fixed for real** - the one Milestone 50 flagged and worked around with a narrow CMake-level skip rather than fixing (`rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_generate_interfaces.cmake`'s own former `rcl_interfaces`+`.srv` `continue()`, now removed by this milestone), TickLE Plan's own next-priority item, at the user's own direct approval. **Root cause** (`tools/typesupport/tickle_typesupport/ros2_cli.py`'s own `generate()`): `resolve.Ros2Resolver`'s `sibling_dir` was computed as plain `os.path.dirname(input_path)` - correct for a `.msg` referencing a same-package sibling `.msg` (both live in the same `msg/` directory), but wrong for a `.srv` doing the identical thing (ROS 2's own `pkg/{msg,srv}/` layout puts a `.srv` one directory away from `msg/`, so the lookup silently missed and fell through to the `-I` search path instead - for `rcl_interfaces`' own real case, a *different*, unpatched copy of `ParameterValue.msg` missing `rcl_interfaces_rmw_tickle.patch`'s own `# @capacity` annotations, surfacing as "auto-derived capacity only supports one trailing array" instead of generating correctly; for the general case with no `-I` fallback available at all, `resolve.UnresolvedTypeError` outright). **Fix**: `sibling_dir` is now always derived as `<input's own package root>/msg` (`os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(input_path))` + `"msg"`) rather than `input_path`'s own immediate parent - correct for both `.msg` (a no-op, since a `.msg`'s own parent already *is* `<pkg>/msg`) and `.srv` (now correctly resolves to `<pkg>/msg`, not `<pkg>/srv`) inputs, matching ROS 2's own actual convention that a nested field is always some other `.msg`, never a `.srv` (ROS 2's grammar doesn't allow a `.srv` to nest another `.srv`). **A real, pre-existing test-fixture gap found while adding coverage for this**: `tests/test_ros2_nested.py`'s own `Leaf.msg`/`Branch.msg` fixtures lived flat in `fixtures_own/` (no `msg/` subdirectory at all) rather than a real `pkg/msg/` layout - happened to still work under the old, buggy `dirname(input_path)` logic (a flat directory *is* its own dirname), but broke under the corrected logic (which now expects a real two-level `pkg/msg/` structure) - moved to `fixtures_own/test_msgs/msg/{Leaf,Branch}.msg`, matching the `nested_arrays_pkg` fixture directory convention already established elsewhere in the same directory; not a regression in the fix itself, a latent inaccuracy in the fixture that the fix's own stricter, more-real-ROS2-shaped assumption finally exposed. **New coverage** (`tests/test_ros2_srv_sibling.py`, `fixtures_own/srv_sibling_pkg/{msg/Leaf.msg, srv/UsesLeaf.srv}` - a real `pkg/msg`+`pkg/srv` split, mirroring `rcl_interfaces`' own `SetParameters.srv`-nests-`msg/Parameter` shape exactly): confirmed to reproduce the exact original failure before the fix (`resolve.UnresolvedTypeError: nested type 'srv_sibling_pkg/Leaf' not found...`, verified by temporarily reverting just the `ros2_cli.py` change and re-running) and pass after it; a second case confirms the same "reuse, don't re-generate a second copy" contract `test_ros2_nested.py`'s own analogous `.msg`-from-`.msg` test already asserts. Full `tools/typesupport` suite (`python3 -m pytest tests/`, 68 tests - not itself wired into any CI workflow currently, a separate pre-existing gap, not addressed here) clean. `rcl_interfaces_rmw_tickle.patch`'s own `.srv` files (`SetParameters` et al.) are not yet exercised end-to-end against a real `rcl_interfaces` build in CI either way - `check-all.yml` still only clones `rcl_interfaces` for `test_msgs`/`test_interface_files`, never builds `rcl_interfaces` itself (the separately-tracked "wire the patch into check-all.yml" follow-up) - so this fix is verified correct at the generator level (real bug, real reproduction, real fix, real regression-free full suite) but the specific `rcl_interfaces` `.srv` payload it unblocks has no real end-to-end CI proof yet, same honest caveat Milestone 50 already carried for its own `.msg`-only scope. | 38, 50 | ✅ real bug fixed at the generator level, full local test suite (68/68) green including a new test that reproduces the original failure before the fix; real CI (`Check all`/`Test all`/`Performance Test`/`rmw_tickle performance`) all confirmed green on the actual push (`d305a3d`) |

same deliberately generous-but-arbitrary default `ListParametersResult.msg`'s own pre-existing annotation already used, about making the package *build*, not a real workload's own batch size), with a new canonical explanation on `SetParameters.srv`'s own header (both limitations, why each exists, that (1) is expected to go away once Milestone 38's array-of-nested-type auto-derivation lands while (2) won't) and short pointer comments on the other five files. **A third, separate real bug, found only once the capacity errors were cleared and the build reached actual compilation**: `rcl_interfaces/msg/Log.msg`'s (and `ParameterEvent.msg`'s) own `builtin_interfaces/Time stamp` field failed with a plain `Time.h: No such file or directory` - true for a real end-user workspace (Milestone 50's own rpi verification presumably built enough of the dependency chain for this to hold), false for `check-all.yml`'s own narrow `--packages-select`, which finds `builtin_interfaces`'s real `.msg` text via the apt-installed package's own share directory (satisfying `_find_on_search_path()`) without ever actually building it with `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c` in this workspace - so nothing ever wrote the `Time.h` the resolver's own contract assumed would already exist.
