| **56** | ✅ **`Arrays.srv`'s own `.msg`/`.srv` base-name header collision, fixed for real (not just worked around)** - TickLE Plan's next-priority item ("test_interface_files 남은 제외 항목 정리"), a low-priority "여유 있으실 때" item, at the user's own direct approval. Milestone 55's own real finding: `test_msgs` has both `msg/BasicTypes.msg` and `srv/BasicTypes.srv`, and `ros2_cli.py`'s own generator wrote each one's own TickLE-side header as a bare `<name>.h/.c` into its own `msg/`/`srv/` output subdirectory - since both directories sit on one real compile unit's own `-I` list simultaneously, the C preprocessor's own same-directory-first quoted-include search silently picked whichever same-named file lived in *that file's own* directory, wrong whenever a `.srv` needed a same-package `.msg`'s own struct (`Arrays.srv`'s own request nesting the real `BasicTypes` message). **Fix**: `ros2_cli.py`'s own `generate()` now writes a `.srv`'s own TickLE-side file as `<name>_srv.h/.c`, never `<name>.h/.c` - a `.srv` is never itself a valid *nested-field target* under ROS 2's own grammar (nothing can nest a service), so nothing outside this one function ever looks a `.srv`'s own TickLE-side name back up the way `resolve.Ros2Resolver` does for a `.msg` - safe to qualify only this side, leaving every `.msg`'s own existing bare-name convention (and `Ros2Resolver`'s own matching `"{msg_name}.h"` computation) completely untouched. **A second, necessary fix found only once this hit a real CMake build, not caught by directly calling `ros2_cli.generate()` from Python alone**: `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_generate_interfaces.cmake`'s own `add_custom_command(OUTPUT ...)` declaration computed the expected output file as plain `${_idl_name}.h/.c` for *both* `.msg` and `.srv` - once the generator itself started actually writing `<name>_srv.h/.c` for a `.srv`, every `.srv` file in the whole project (not just the colliding ones) failed with a hard "No such file or directory" (CMake's own declared-vs-actual-output mismatch, not a lint issue) - fixed by computing the same `_srv`-suffixed stem there too, gated on `_parent_folder STREQUAL "srv"`. `Arrays.srv` un-excluded from `test_msgs_rmw_tickle.patch`'s own `EXCLUDE REGEX` accordingly (it never needed array-of-nested-type support at all, fixed-`[3]`-only same as `Arrays.msg` - the header collision was the entire reason it stayed excluded). Verified: full `tools/typesupport` pytest suite (69/69, no regressions - `test_ros2_srv_sibling.py`'s own `Leaf.h`/`.c`-absence assertions, the closest existing coverage to this exact collision shape, still pass unchanged since they check for the *nested* type's own file, unaffected by this fix); a from-scratch local `colcon build --packages-select test_interface_files builtin_interfaces test_msgs rcl_interfaces --allow-overriding rcl_interfaces builtin_interfaces` (all three patches applied, `Arrays.srv` now included) builds clean, 4/4 packages, zero failures - confirmed directly via the generated request/response struct names now correctly carrying the `_srv` qualifier (`struct Empty_srvRequest`, etc.), not just that nothing errored. **Real CI (`Check all`) confirmed** (commit `171e288`): the real log shows `69 passed in 7.49s`, `Finished <<< test_msgs [1min 29s]` (`Arrays.srv` now genuinely included and building), `Finished <<< rcl_interfaces [1min 30s]`, and `100% tests passed, 0 tests failed out of 14` for `test_rmw_implementation` - unaffected; `Test all`/`Performance Test`/`rmw_tickle performance` all green too. | 38, 41, 42, 52, 54, 55 | ✅ real CI confirmed `Arrays.srv` builds clean and the conformance suite (14/14) is unaffected - the header-collision bug is fixed for real, not just excluded around |

| **32** | ✅ **`LIVELINESS` `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC`** - the last of the three QoS-parity items "TickLE Plan"'s own review flagged (Milestone 28), lowest priority behind RxO and `LIVELINESS_LOST`. `MANUAL_BY_PARTICIPANT`/`_BY_NODE` were in scope per the original ask, but turned out not to exist in this rmw's own `rmw_qos_policy_liveliness_t` at all - removed from the real rmw spec at some point before this ROS 2 distro (`lyrical`) - so this milestone is `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC` only, the one manual kind actually left to support. New `rmw_publisher_assert_liveliness()` (`rmw_publisher.c`) is the real, working entry point rclcpp needs - previously absent entirely (a latent, if rarely-hit, dlsym gap: harmless as long as nothing ever called it, since `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC` itself was rejected at QoS validation, but a real gap regardless). **`rmw_qos.c`**: accepts `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC` alongside `AUTOMATIC` now. **`rmw_tickle_publisher_t`** gains `liveliness_lease_ns` (0 = `AUTOMATIC`, Milestone 30's own original behavior; non-zero = this Publisher's own manual lease, falling back to the same `rmw_qos.c` floor when left at `RMW_QOS_LIVELINESS_LEASE_DURATION_DEFAULT`), `last_asserted_ns` (atomic, refreshed only by an explicit `rmw_publisher_assert_liveliness()` call - unlike `poll_thread_last_return_ns`, nothing refreshes this automatically), and `liveliness_lost_latched` (a per-Publisher edge-trigger latch, since each manual Publisher's own lease lapses independently, unlike `AUTOMATIC`'s one shared node-wide latch). **A real regression found and fixed while building this** (documented here since it directly shaped the final design, not left to a future milestone to rediscover): the first version of this checked both `AUTOMATIC` and manual publishers from one combined function called unconditionally every watchdog cycle, and this broke Milestone 30's own already-working `AUTOMATIC` case - found by `test_liveliness_lost_watchdog.c` failing deterministically (not flaky, reproduced 3/3 local runs) as soon as `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC` support was added. Root cause: Milestone 30's own design relied on a specific ordering guarantee - `node_stale` is computed lock-free, and `node->mutex` is only ever locked (an operation that may legitimately block for as long as the mutex is held, e.g. a real hang) *after* `node_stale` is already known true, so the action taken once unblocked never has to re-derive anything from a timestamp that's gone stale itself. The combined function broke this by locking unconditionally *every* cycle (needed to enumerate manual Publishers) - including cycles where `node_stale` was still false, meaning the very first cycle during a real hang would block on the mutex using a premature "not stale yet" snapshot, and by the time it finally unblocked (once the hang ended), `poll_thread` had already raced ahead and refreshed `poll_thread_last_return_ns` to a fresh value, erasing the staleness signal before the watchdog's next cycle could observe it. **Fix**: split back into two functions - `mark_automatic_publishers_lost()` (verbatim Milestone 30 behavior, only ever locked from the exact cycle `node_stale && !already_lost` transitions true) and a new `check_manual_publishers_lost()` (checked every cycle, independent of `node_stale`, since `last_asserted_ns` has no equivalent auto-healing behavior - nothing races to refresh it the instant a lock frees, only an explicit application call does that). The second, related fix within `check_manual_publishers_lost()` itself: a plain `pthread_mutex_trylock()` turned out to lose to `poll_thread` almost every single call (measured directly - added temporary debug logging, confirmed empirically before writing the real fix, not guessed) - `poll_thread_main()` holds `node->mutex` for the duration of every `tt_Node_poll()` call and releases it for only `RMW_TICKLE_POLL_THREAD_YIELD_NS` (1us) before relocking, and a bare `trylock()` isn't a queued waiter the way a blocking `pthread_mutex_lock()` is (no futex wake fairness), so it almost never lands in that 1us gap. Switched to `pthread_mutex_timedlock()` with a short, bounded `RMW_TICKLE_WATCHDOG_MANUAL_CHECK_TIMEOUT_NS` (10ms) instead - long enough to reliably win under ordinary `poll_thread` contention (a real queued wait, unlike `trylock()`), short enough that a genuine long hang still can't block this specific check for more than that bound. **New tests** (`test_liveliness_lost_watchdog.c`, extended): a `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC` Publisher that's never asserted goes `LIVELINESS_LOST` on its own schedule, no external stall needed (unlike `AUTOMATIC`'s own mutex-holding test) - proving the manual obligation is checked independently of `poll_thread` health; a second `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC` Publisher that calls `rmw_publisher_assert_liveliness()` faster than its own lease never goes lost, even well past when it would have without those calls - proving the assertion API actually keeps a manual Publisher alive, not just that a neglected one goes lost. **`test_qos.c`** updated: its own `MANUAL_BY_TOPIC still rejected` case now asserts `RMW_RET_OK`, for every entity kind, matching `DEADLINE`'s/`LIVELINESS_LEASE_DURATION`'s own already-established "validated for every kind, monitored for Publisher/Subscription only" pattern. Verified: `colcon build`/`colcon test` against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install - 12/12 green (suite runtime ~13s, up from ~5s - the two new real-time manual-liveliness cases' own cost, plus confirmed stable across repeated direct runs, not just the one colcon pass); `clang-tidy`/`clang-format` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed file (one real finding, fixed: `clock_gettime(CLOCK_REALTIME, ...)`'s own `misc-include-cleaner` warning, same `NOLINT` precedent `rmw_wait_set.c` already established for the identical call). | 21, 30 | ✅ done |

| **33** | ✅ **Remaining rmw API surface, first slice** - "TickLE Plan"'s own backlog (superseded-priority list, item 5), picking up Milestone 15's own "Remaining, not chased down this pass" note. Scoped to the smallest-effort, direct-reuse pieces first (per that note's own listing) - the larger ones (`rmw_get_topic_names_and_types()`/service-names-and-types family, `rmw_*_get_network_flow_endpoints()`, `rmw_take_dynamic_message()`/`rmw_serialization_support_init()`, `rmw_*_set_on_new_*_callback()`/`rmw_event_set_callback()`, content-filtered topics) are explicitly **not** attempted here, left as open follow-ons - see their own sizing note below. **`rmw_count_clients()`/`rmw_count_services()`** (`rmw_graph.c`): previously missing symbols entirely; identical shape to the pre-existing `rmw_count_publishers()`/`_subscribers()`, just scanning `tt_KIND_SERVICE_CLIENT`/`_SERVER` instead of the two topic-side kinds - `count_matching()`/`count_matching_locked()` already scan both local endpoints and remote discovery regardless of which kind is passed, so no new mechanism was needed. **`rmw_publisher_count_matched_subscriptions()`/`rmw_subscription_count_matched_publishers()`** (`rmw_graph.c`): also previously missing; new shared `count_matching_via_node_impl()` (the interrupt-then-lock-then-scan-then-unlock sequence `count_matching()` already had, generalized to take an `rmw_tickle_node_t*` directly) lets these reuse the exact same same-topic-name-and-kind computation `rmw_count_publishers()`/`_subscribers()` use, scoped to "the topic this one Publisher/Subscription is on" - deliberately **not** QoS-compatibility-filtered (would need re-deriving each remote match's own offered/requested bits via `struct tt_DiscoveredEntity.qos` and comparing, Milestone 31's own RxO machinery), matching `rmw_count_publishers()`/`_subscribers()`'s own pre-existing unfiltered scope rather than introducing an inconsistency between two otherwise-identical counting functions - tracked as a shared, not-yet-closed refinement for both. **`rmw_feature_supported()`** (new `rmw_features.c`): the one entry point in this whole file that takes no node/publisher/subscription at all (`rmw/features.h`) - a pure `switch` over the four `rmw_feature_t` values. Returns `true` for both `RMW_FEATURE_MESSAGE_INFO_PUBLICATION_SEQUENCE_NUMBER`/`_RECEPTION_SEQUENCE_NUMBER` (verified genuinely correct, not just present: `rmw_tickle_queued_message_t.publication_sequence_number`/`.reception_sequence_number`, `rmw_tickle_c/rmw_tickle.h`, are filled from a real wire seq_no and a real local monotonic counter respectively, both already exercised by `test_events.c`'s own message-info checks), `false` for the two dynamic-type-related ones (no dynamic typesupport mechanism exists in `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`/`_cpp` at all). **`rmw_publisher_wait_for_all_acked()`** (`rmw_publisher.c`): previously missing; `BEST_EFFORT` returns `RMW_RET_OK` immediately (correct per spec - nothing to acknowledge for `BEST_EFFORT` at all), `RELIABLE` returns a documented, honest `RMW_RET_UNSUPPORTED` rather than an approximate proxy (e.g. "every `reliable_cache` entry's own retry count is currently 0") that wouldn't actually mean what this function promises - a real implementation needs Publisher-side ack-completion tracking TickLE core doesn't have yet (today's ack bookkeeping runs the other way: each reliable Subscriber owns its own `ack_seq_no`/`received_bitmap`, a Publisher only ever reacts to incoming ACKNACKs via `process_acknack()`, with no aggregated "which currently-matched peers have fully caught up" view of its own) - tracked as an open follow-on, not solved here. **New `tests/test_graph.c` extension**: `rmw_count_clients()`/`_services()` exercised the same way the pre-existing publisher/subscriber counting is, via a real `tt_Client`/`tt_Server` pair built directly on `tickle_node` (new fake request/response encode/decode/free stubs, mirroring the file's own existing fake topic-data stubs). **New `tests/test_rmw_api_surface.c`** (needs a real, *matched* `rmw_publisher_t`/`rmw_subscription_t` pair on the same topic, unlike `test_graph.c`'s own deliberately-raw-`tt_Publisher`/`tt_Subscriber` approach - hence its own file): `rmw_feature_supported()`'s own four cases (no node needed at all); a Publisher with no Subscription sees 0 matches, gains 1 once a same-topic Subscription is created, and an unrelated-topic Subscription sees neither side of that pair; `rmw_publisher_wait_for_all_acked()`'s own `BEST_EFFORT`-returns-OK and `RELIABLE`-returns-`UNSUPPORTED` cases. Verified: `colcon build`/`colcon test` against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install - 14/14 green (up from 12, the two new/extended test binaries); `clang-tidy`/`clang-format` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed/new file (one real finding, fixed: `test_rmw_api_surface.c`'s own two `999`-sentinel magic numbers, extracted into a named `UNSET_COUNT_SENTINEL` constant with a doc comment explaining why 999 specifically, not `0`); `make test`/`sanitize`/`platform/freertos` unaffected (no TickLE-core changes this milestone - purely `rmw_tickle`-layer reuse of existing mechanisms). **Explicitly deferred, sized for a future pass** (per the scoping review this milestone's own plan was based on): the "names and types" family (`rmw_get_topic_names_and_types()`, `rmw_get_service_names_and_types()`, the four `*_by_node` variants) and `rmw_get_publishers_info_by_topic()`/`_subscriptions_info_by_topic()` - medium effort, needs the same dual local-endpoint/remote-discovery scan this file's own functions already established, but building real `rmw_names_and_types_array_t`/`rmw_topic_endpoint_info_array_t` allocator-based results instead of a plain count; `rmw_*_get_network_flow_endpoints()` - no meaningful "flow endpoint" concept exists below UDP unicast/broadcast in TickLE, likely not worth building; `rmw_take_dynamic_message()`/`rmw_serialization_support_init()` - an entirely separate dynamic-typesupport mechanism `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`/`_cpp` don't participate in at all; `rmw_*_set_on_new_*_callback()`/`rmw_event_set_callback()` - medium-large, needs real event-callback-on-poll-thread wiring into the existing background-poll-thread-plus-per-node-mutex locking model, not just a data query; `rmw_subscription_*_content_filter()` - would need real filter-expression support added to the wire protocol itself. | 6, 15 | ✅ done |

| **34** | ✅ **Multiple ROS 2 nodes per process** - "TickLE Plan"'s own backlog (superseded-priority list, item 3), superseding this same milestone's own earlier "design pass only" finding below the fold in this row's history. **Root finding that unblocked it**: DDS itself has no "Node" concept at all - `rclcpp::Node` is a pure rcl/rmw-layer name/namespace grouping with no independent DDS entity of its own (real ROS 2 tracks node-to-entity ownership via `rmw_dds_common`'s own `ParticipantEntitiesInfo` metadata topic, layered *on top of* DDS, not a core mechanism) - confirmed by reading `test_rmw_implementation/test/test_graph_api.cpp` itself: `TestGraphAPI.count_clients_and_services` (the one case actually needing a second same-process node) never once inspects transport identity, only name/namespace bookkeeping. This means the earlier "design pass only" finding's whole premise - that co-resident nodes need distinct transport identities, and therefore either distinct ports (breaks discovery) or one shared port via `SO_REUSEPORT` (silently misroutes unicast traffic, see that finding's own detailed writeup) - was solving a problem multiple ROS 2 nodes per process never actually has: no new socket, discovery instance, or wire change was needed at all, just multiple name labels sharing the one transport participant a context already has. **Design**: `struct tt_Node`, `poll_thread`, `node_mutex` (renamed from `mutex`), `watchdog_thread`, `poll_thread_last_return_ns`, and `struct tt_Discovery discovery` all promoted from `rmw_tickle_node_t` (one per logical node, previously) up to `rmw_tickle_context_impl_t` (one per process/context) - `rmw_tickle_node_t` itself shrinks to a thin `rmw_node_t`/`context_impl` pointer/`allocator` wrapper. A new reference-counted registry (`atomic_int node_count`, allocator-owned `rmw_tickle_node_t** nodes`, both guarded by a **new, dedicated** `registry_mutex` - deliberately *not* reusing `wait_mutex`, a different lock-nesting contract guarding a different concern) gates `start_shared_tickle_node()`/`stop_shared_tickle_node()` on the 0→1/1→0 transition: `rmw_create_node()` starts the shared `tt_Node` only for the first logical node in a context, later ones just register; `rmw_destroy_node()` only tears it down once the *last* one goes. **A real regression found and fixed by real CI, not local testing** (documented here since it directly shaped the final design): the first version of this rejected a second `rmw_create_node()` call sharing an already-registered `(name, namespace)` pair outright, matching this project's own general "reject explicitly" precedent - but this broke the very conformance suite this milestone set out to unblock, all 15 of `TestGraphAPI`'s own `TEST_F()`s at once, not just `count_clients_and_services`: `test_graph_api.cpp`'s own `TestGraphAPI::SetUp()` deliberately creates its second node with the *exact same* name/namespace as the first, and real rmw/DDS has no node-name-uniqueness constraint at all (node names are an rcl/ROS-graph-layer convention, never enforced at the rmw layer itself) - so rejecting the duplicate was simply wrong, not a defensible design choice this project's own philosophy happened to disagree with. Fixed by removing the check entirely; two (or more) `rmw_tickle_node_t`s sharing one `(name, namespace)` are now fully legal, distinguished only by pointer identity (`unregister_node()`'s own lookup key). New `owning_node_name`/`owning_node_namespace` fields (owned `rcutils_strdup()` copies, not back-pointers into the node, to avoid lifetime coupling) added to all four entity structs (`rmw_tickle_publisher_t`/`_subscriber_t`/`_client_t`/`_service_t`) - not consumed by anything yet, laying groundwork for a future by-node graph-query family. **Side effect closing part of Milestone 6's own long-open gap**: `rmw_get_node_names()`/`rmw_get_node_names_with_enclaves()` now enumerate every logical node registered under `context_impl->nodes[]`, not just the one `rmw_node_t` handle a caller happened to pass in - this process's own nodes only, still never a remote process's (TickLE's wire protocol still has no node-name concept at all, unchanged by this milestone). **Conformance suite: attempted, reverted, a real new gap found instead of an easy win**: unskipping `test_graph_api.cpp`'s own rmw_tickle-specific `GTEST_SKIP()` (`.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/`) looked like the natural next step now that a second same-process node works, but real CI showed the whole fixture still can't pass, for two separate reasons - neither one this milestone's own scope: (1) most of `TestGraphAPI`'s 15 `TEST_F()`s exercise the "names and types"/`*_by_node`/`*_info_by_topic` function family, which Milestone 33 already explicitly deferred as its own separate, larger follow-on, not something a multi-node fix touches; (2) `count_clients_and_services` itself - the one case this milestone's own design was actually aimed at - hit a genuinely new, deeper problem: it creates two *independent* `rmw_service_t` servers for the *exact same* service name from two different node handles, expecting `rmw_count_services()` to report 2, matching how real DDS lets multiple independent entities share a name. TickLE's own `tt_Node_create_server()` computes its wire `endpoint_id` as `tt_hash_id(service->name, endpoint_name)` - a pure hash of the name, with no participant/registration disambiguator at all - so a single `tt_Node` can only ever host *one* local Server for a given service name, full stop. This constraint predates Milestone 34 entirely (it's a `tickle.c` wire-level design decision, unrelated to how many rmw nodes share a `tt_Node`), but promoting every logical node in a process onto one shared `tt_Node` is what first made it reachable within a single process. A real fix needs a TickLE-core wire-protocol change (some per-registration uniquifier folded into `endpoint_id`, not just the name) - out of scope for a multi-node-per-process milestone that otherwise needed zero wire changes, and not attempted here, matching this project's own "reject/design explicitly rather than getting it subtly wrong" precedent (the original SO_REUSEPORT finding earlier in this same milestone's own history). The conformance patch's fixture-wide skip is restored, with its own comment updated to explain both reasons instead of the old "rmw_tickle only supports one node" reasoning that's no longer true. **New `test_multi_node.c`**: two `rmw_create_node()` calls on one context share the exact same `tt_Node`/`context_impl` (pointer-identity checks, not just equal-looking state); `rmw_get_node_names()` reports both; a *third* node sharing the first one's exact `(name, namespace)` is accepted, not rejected (the case real CI caught missing); destroying a sibling leaves the shared `poll_thread` running as long as at least one logical node remains, and only the very last one destroyed actually stops it. Verified: `colcon build`/`colcon test` against a real ROS 2 (`lyrical`) install - 16/16 green (up from 14, this new test); `clang-tidy`/`clang-format` against the real `compile_commands.json` clean on every changed/new file (two real findings, fixed: `register_node()`'s/`rmw_context_fini()`'s own `rmw_tickle_node_t**`→`void*` reallocate/deallocate calls needing an explicit cast (`bugprone-multi-level-implicit-pointer-conversion`), and `rmw_get_node_names()`/`_with_enclaves()`'s own cleanup-on-error paths needing a small shared `fini_string_array_ignore_result()` helper since GCC's `warn_unused_result` on `rcutils_string_array_fini()` isn't satisfied by a plain `(void)`-cast on the call itself, only by capturing its return value first); `make test`/`sanitize`/`platform/freertos` unaffected (no TickLE-core changes this milestone - `struct tt_Node` itself needed no changes at all, only how many of it an `rmw_tickle` process creates); real CI (`Test all`, `Check all`) - `Check all`'s first push caught the duplicate-rejection regression above (all 15 `TestGraphAPI` cases failing at once), and its second push caught the `count_clients_and_services` endpoint-id collision above (the same 15, for the two separate reasons documented there); a third push, with the conformance patch's fixture-wide skip restored, confirmed both green. | 2, 6 | ✅ done |

| **35** | ✅ **Multiple local endpoints of one kind may share a name** - a real TickLE-core (`tickle.c`) gap Milestone 34's own `count_clients_and_services` investigation surfaced, not something Milestone 34 itself could fix (it needed no wire/socket changes at all; this one is a real protocol-adjacent data-structure change). **Root cause**: `add_endpoint_to_node()` rejected a second local endpoint (Publisher, Subscriber, Client, or Server) sharing an already-registered `(kind, id)` outright as "Duplicate endpoint" - a purely local table-uniqueness assumption, never actually a wire-protocol requirement, that predates Milestone 34 entirely (even a single rmw node creating two Subscriptions to the same topic already hit it) but was invisible before Milestone 34 made "more than one logical entity in one process" reachable at all. Real DDS lets multiple independent Publishers/Subscribers/Clients/Servers share a topic/service name; TickLE's own `endpoint_id = tt_hash_id(name)` (deliberately a pure function of the name alone - see its own doc comment, `encoding.c` - the mechanism by which two otherwise-unacquainted nodes agree on "the same" topic/service with zero negotiation) was never itself the problem, only the local table's assumption that this id had to be unique. **Fix**: `add_endpoint_to_node()` no longer rejects a `(kind, id)` collision, only an exact duplicate *pointer* (a real double-create bug, still caught). `find_endpoint()` keeps its existing single-match contract unchanged (still returns exactly one match, deterministically the oldest still-registered one, per its own probe-order doc comment) for callers that route a reply/retransmission to one specific instance - `process_callrequest()`/`process_callresponse()`/`process_acknack()` - since the wire protocol has no per-instance id beyond the name hash, so picking a specific one of several identically-named local endpoints is genuinely ambiguous at the wire level, not a gap local logic could close (matches real DDS's own undefined-which-one semantics for redundant same-name entities; RELIABLE QoS between two duplicate-named local Publishers specifically has an accepted, documented correctness gap here - two independent Publishers interleaving DATA under one wire id would corrupt a remote reliable Subscriber's own seq_no tracking regardless of which one answers a given ACKNACK, not attempted to be solved without a real wire-format change). New `for_each_endpoint()` (same linear-probe walk as `find_endpoint()`, continued past a match instead of returning immediately) is used instead wherever reaching *every* local match is the actual correctness requirement: `process_data()`'s own Subscriber delivery (extracted into `deliver_data_to_subscriber()`, now called once per matching local Subscriber - each with its own independent RxO check, reliable-ack state, and callback, exactly as if it had received its own private copy, matching real DDS pub/sub fan-out) and `process_heartbeat()`'s own Subscriber reliable-baseline update (`inform_subscriber_of_heartbeat()`, symmetric with `process_data()`'s fan-out - both needed together, since a Subscriber that gets DATA from a fanned-out Publisher but never gets the matching Heartbeat would fall back to the slower DATA-only reliable bootstrap), plus `decode_update_entities()`'s own two peer-registration branches (`register_subscriber_peer_on_publisher()`/`register_server_peer_on_client()`) - a newly-discovered remote peer now registers against *every* local Publisher/Client sharing that name, not just the first, so a second local Publisher (now legal to create at all) can actually acquire peers of its own instead of existing locally with nothing to talk to. `process_data()`'s own pre-existing "decode failure returns false" contract (`test_publish_subscribe.c`'s own `test_process_data_decode_failure_is_reported`) is preserved via a `decode_failed` out-flag on the new fan-out's own shared context struct, set by any matching Subscriber's own failed decode. **New tests**: `tests/test_duplicate_endpoints.c` (new file - the only TickLE-core test file that reaches `tt_Node_create_publisher()`/`_subscriber()`/`_client()`/`_server()` through their own real public entry points at all, every other existing test constructs its structs by hand, bypassing `add_endpoint_to_node()` entirely) - two Publishers/Subscribers/Clients/Servers each sharing a name all succeed; registering the exact same pointer twice still fails; destroying one duplicate leaves its sibling registered; a discovery UPDATE registers a peer on every matching local Publisher, not just one. `tests/test_publish_subscribe.c` gains `test_process_data_fans_out_to_every_matching_subscriber` - two local Subscribers on one topic both get their own independent delivery from one arriving DATA submessage. Verified: `make test`/`make sanitize` (ASan+UBSan)/`make -C platform/freertos` all green; `clang-format`/`clang-tidy` (bare invocation, matching this project's own TickLE-core discipline) clean on every changed/new file. **Follow-up (same day)**: moved `test_graph_api.cpp`'s own rmw_tickle skip from fixture-wide (`SetUp()`) to per-`TEST_F()` - `SetUp()`/`TearDown()` are unconditional again (matching real upstream exactly), and each of the other 14 `TEST_F()`s gets its own `GTEST_SKIP()`, each with its own accurate reason found by actually reading its own real CI failure, not assumed: 2 need `rmw_get_node_names()`'s own still-missing non-zero-array rejection (`rmw_check_zero_rmw_string_array()`), 4 need `rmw_count_publishers()`/`_subscribers()`/`_clients()`/`_services()`'s own still-missing `rmw_validate_full_topic_name()` call, and 8 need Milestone 33's own already-deferred "names and types" family. `count_clients_and_services` itself - the one case this whole effort was aimed at - now runs unconditionally, no skip at all. Verified: patch regenerated via a real `git diff` against a pristine upstream clone (not hand-edited unified-diff text - Milestone 34's own patch-editing mistakes earlier this same day, corrupted blank-context-lines and a wrong hunk-header line count, both came from typing diff syntax by hand) and confirmed `git apply --check`/`git apply` clean on a fresh clone; real CI (`Check all`) confirmed the other 14 cases genuinely `[  SKIPPED ]` and `TestGraphAPI.count_clients_and_services` itself `[       OK ]` - the concrete external validation this whole multi-node effort was aimed at. | 34 | ✅ done |

| **36** | ✅ **Two `rmw_graph.c` argument-validation gaps, found by this same session's own conformance-suite skip audit** - "TickLE Plan"'s own prioritized backlog, item 1: cheap, well-scoped, close them before the bigger names-and-types follow-on (Milestone 33's own deferral). **Gap 1**: `rmw_get_node_names()`/`rmw_get_node_names_with_enclaves()` never rejected a caller-supplied output array that already held data (not freshly `rcutils_get_zero_initialized_string_array()`'d) - silently overwriting/leaking it instead. Fixed by calling `rmw_check_zero_rmw_string_array()` (`rmw/sanity_checks.h`) on each array before touching any of them, converting its own `RMW_RET_ERROR` to the `RMW_RET_INVALID_ARGUMENT` upstream's own test expects; ordered so a rejected array leaves every *other* array genuinely untouched (`rmw_get_node_names_with_enclaves()` checks `enclaves` before ever calling into `rmw_get_node_names()`, which itself checks `node_names` before `node_namespaces`) - matches `test_rmw_implementation`'s own exact per-argument isolation expectations. **Gap 2**: `rmw_count_publishers()`/`_subscribers()`/`_clients()`/`_services()` (all four share `count_matching()`) never validated their own `topic_name`/`service_name` argument's format at all - an empty string, one with spaces, or any other malformed name silently succeeded instead of being rejected. Fixed via `rmw_validate_full_topic_name()` (`rmw/validate_full_topic_name.h`), the exact same "validate, then check the `_VALID` sentinel, `RMW_SET_ERROR_MSG` the description string on failure" shape `rmw_create_node()` already established for `rmw_validate_node_name()`/`rmw_validate_namespace()` (Milestone 2) - service names validated as topic names too, matching how real rmw implementations treat a service as a topic pair under the hood. Both gaps were things this session's own conformance-suite investigation (Milestone 34/35's own `count_clients_and_services` work) found and initially only *documented* as accepted, skipped-around limitations - fixed for real here instead, per TickLE Plan's own priority ordering. **Conformance suite**: the 6 `TEST_F()`s these two gaps were blocking (`get_node_names_with_bad_arguments`, `get_node_names_with_enclaves_with_bad_arguments`, `count_publishers/subscribers/clients/services_with_bad_arguments`) had their own per-test `GTEST_SKIP()`s (added earlier this same day, Milestone 34's own follow-up) removed now that the underlying gaps are actually fixed - only the 8 genuinely-still-unimplemented "names and types" cases remain skipped. Verified: `colcon build`/`colcon test` - 16/16 green, no regressions; `clang-tidy`/`clang-format` clean; patch regenerated the same pristine-clone-diff way Milestone 34's own follow-up established (not hand-edited), confirmed `git apply --check`/`git apply` clean on a fresh clone; real CI (`Check all`, `Test all`) confirmed green - all 7 (the 6 unskipped here plus `count_clients_and_services` itself) show `[       OK ]`, the remaining 8 genuinely `[  SKIPPED ]`. | 34, 35 | ✅ done |

| **37** | ✅ **The "names and types" function family** - Milestone 33's own explicitly-deferred larger follow-on, TickLE Plan's own priority item 2: `rmw_get_topic_names_and_types()`/`_service_names_and_types()`, the four `*_by_node()` variants (`rmw_get_subscriber/publisher/service/client_names_and_types_by_node()`), and `rmw_get_publishers_info_by_topic()`/`_subscriptions_info_by_topic()` - the last 8 `TestGraphAPI` cases this whole multi-session effort was working toward. **Design**: reuses Milestone 6's own dual-scan pattern (local `tickle_node.endpoints[]` + remote `discovery.entities[]`) throughout, exactly like `count_matching()` already does - no new TickLE-core mechanism needed. A local `tt_Endpoint` has no type-name or owning-node field of its own, so both are recovered via the same `offsetof()` reverse-cast idiom every discovery/data callback in this package already uses, reaching the concrete `rmw_tickle_publisher_t`/`_subscriber_t`/`_client_t`/`_service_t` wrapper to read `topic.name`/`service.name` (the real ROS type name, generated-code string literals - mirrors `tickle.c`'s own private `endpoint_type_name()`, not reachable from `rmw_graph.c`) and Milestone 34's own `owning_node_name`/`_namespace` fields (consumed for the first time here). A remote `tt_DiscoveredEntity` already carries its own `.type` string on the wire (`encode_update_entities()`'s own `endpoint_type_name()` call, unchanged) but never a node name at all - TickLE's wire protocol simply has none (this file's own long-standing module doc comment) - so the four `*_by_node()` queries can only ever truthfully answer for a node in *this* process (checked against `context_impl->nodes[]`, Milestone 34's own registry); anything else is an honest `RMW_RET_NODE_NAME_NON_EXISTENT`, not a guess. `rmw_get_topic_names_and_types()`/`_service_names_and_types()` merge two kinds each (Publisher+Subscriber, Server+Client - "for which a publisher and/or a subscription exists" per the header's own doc comment) and deduplicate by `(name, type)` pair before grouping into the one-`names[]`-entry-with-a-`types[]`-array-alongside-it shape `rmw_names_and_types_t` needs (two or more endpoints announcing the exact same name and type - now routine after Milestone 35 - contribute only one entry, not several). `rmw_get_publishers_info_by_topic()`/`_subscriptions_info_by_topic()` instead give every individual matching endpoint instance its own row, gid-encoded the same `(node_id, endpoint_id)` scheme `rmw_get_gid_for_publisher()` already established; a remote entity's own `node_name`/`node_namespace` are honestly `""` (empty, not NULL - the setters require a real C string) and its `qos_profile` starts from `rmw_qos_profile_unknown` with only `reliability`/`durability` overridden from the two bits TickLE's own wire protocol actually carries (`struct tt_DiscoveredEntity.qos`) - matches this API's own documented allowance ("the only QoS policies guaranteed to be shared during discovery are the ones that participate in endpoint matching"). All scanning and the resulting `rcutils_strdup()`/`rmw_topic_endpoint_info_set_*()` calls happen under one held `context_impl->node_mutex` critical section per query - the raw `char*`/`char[]` pointers gathered from `tickle_node.endpoints[]`/`discovery.entities[]` are only valid as long as nothing concurrent (the poll thread, a destroy call) can mutate those tables, so the lock has to stay held all the way through, not just for the scan itself. **New test**: `tests/test_names_and_types.c` (new file - needs a real, matched `rmw_publisher_t`/`rmw_subscription_t` pair *and* a real `rmw_client_t`/`rmw_service_t` pair, since `owning_node_name` only gets populated by the real `rmw_create_*()` entry points, not `test_graph.c`'s own deliberately-raw `tt_Publisher`/`tt_Subscriber` approach - same reasoning as `test_rmw_api_surface.c`'s own separate file; builds a fake `rosidl_service_type_support_t` the same way `test_rmw_api_surface.c` already builds a fake message one) - exercises all 8 functions against a real topic and a real service, confirms deduplication (a Pub+Sub pair on one topic reports it once, not twice), confirms by-node filtering against this process's own node, confirms `RMW_RET_NODE_NAME_NON_EXISTENT` for a genuinely unknown name, confirms `rmw_get_publishers_info_by_topic()`'s own real node_name/node_namespace/topic_type/endpoint_type population, and confirms an unmatched topic returns a real empty array rather than an error (the header's own documented "names of non-existent topics are allowed"). **Conformance suite**: the patch's own `test_graph_api.cpp` section (`.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/`) is removed *entirely* - the file needed after these fixes is byte-identical to pristine upstream (confirmed via `git diff` against a fresh clone), the first upstream test file in this whole patch set to reach that state. Verified: `colcon build`/`colcon test` green (`test_names_and_types` new, 0 regressions); `clang-tidy`/`clang-format` clean on every changed/new file (one real finding, fixed: `build_names_and_types()`'s own cognitive complexity over threshold, resolved by extracting `name_already_seen()`/`count_types_for_name()`/`fill_types_for_name()` - the same three tiny loops it used to repeat inline); patch regenerated via the pristine-clone-diff method (`git apply --check`/a real apply both clean on a fresh clone); real CI (`Test all`, `Check all`) confirmed green - all 15 `TestGraphAPI` cases now show `[       OK ]`, zero skipped, up from 1/15 at the start of this whole multi-node investigation. | 6, 33, 34, 35, 36 | ✅ done |
| **38** | 🔶 **Nested-message support in `rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`'s real ROS 2 pipeline (`ros2_cli.py`)** - TickLE Plan's own priority item 3, and an explicit user requirement alongside it: confirm whether TickLE core itself also needs changes to support nested types. **TickLE core finding: no changes needed at all.** The `tt_DATA_ENCODE_SIZE`/`tt_DATA_ENCODE`/`tt_DATA_DECODE`/`tt_DATA_FREE` callback contract (`include/tickle/tickle.h`) was already a fully general, schema-agnostic "opaque struct in, byte buffer out" interface, already used recursively in already-shipped code (`Image.c` calling `std_msgs__Header_encode()` for its own nested `header` field) - the only real ceiling is the pre-existing, shape-agnostic `tt_MAX_BUFFER_LENGTH` single-datagram budget, already enforced via a `_Static_assert` on every generated struct regardless of nesting. **The real gap was entirely in the Python code generator** (`tools/typesupport/tickle_typesupport`), specifically `ros2_cli.py` - the actual ROS 2 CMake-invoked entry point - hard-coding `resolver=None` at its own `adapt.adapt_message()`/`adapt_service()` call, unconditionally rejecting every nested field even though `cli.py`'s own `-I`-based pipeline for TickLE's own bundled examples already supported one (`Image.msg` nesting `std_msgs/Header`, golden-tested). **Naming-convention conflict found and resolved before writing any fix** (not discovered via a failing test): `adapt_message()`'s own top-level convention names a message's struct `f"{name}Data"` (`BasicTypesData`), but the pre-existing `resolve.Resolver` (used by `cli.py`, unchanged) names a resolved nested struct `f"{pkg}__{msg}"` (`std_msgs__Header`) and writes a brand-new file for it - wrong for `ros2_cli.py`'s real use case, where a nested field's own type (e.g. `test_msgs/BasicTypes` nested from `test_msgs/Nested`) is *also* independently top-level-generated by the exact same CMake extension's own per-`.msg` loop, already producing its own, differently-named, type-incompatible `struct BasicTypesData` - naively reusing `Resolver`'s convention would produce a second struct that could never interoperate with the first. **Fix**: a new `resolve.Ros2Resolver` resolves a nested reference to an independently-generated sibling by reusing that sibling's own `f"{msg}Data"` struct and writing no second file for it at all (falling back to the old `Resolver`/`render_nested()` mechanism, WITH file-writing, only for TickLE's two small bundled builtins, `std_msgs/Header`/`builtin_interfaces/Time`, which are never independently top-level-generated anywhere); new `model.WireStruct.header_name`/`ros_pkg_name`/`ros_type_name` fields (populated by both resolvers) let `render.py`'s `_nested_includes()` and `ros2_adapter.py`'s `ros2_nested_struct_name()` recover the real file name and the real ROS `(pkg, type)` name once `c_name` alone stopped being enough to imply either. **A second, genuinely separate bug found only once a real compiled round-trip test was written** (not caught by `adapt.py`/`model.py` accepting the field without raising, nor by the existing offline `test_ros2_adapter.py` coverage, which never calls a nested field's own `__to_tickle`/`__from_tickle` through a second, independently-generated translation unit): `ros2_adapter.render_adapter()`'s generated `.c` file called a nested field's sibling `<ros_name>__to_tickle()`/`__from_tickle()` without ever including that sibling's own generated header declaring them - compiled with only an implicit-declaration warning, not an error, so nothing local caught it either; fixed via a new `nested_adapter_includes()` (mirrors the existing but, it turns out, never-called `nested_ros_includes()`) wired into `render_adapter()`'s own source includes. **A third, pre-existing but previously-latent bug found while writing this milestone's own new test**: `render.py`'s `_expand()` (empy-based) failed with a spurious `em.Error: interpreter stdout proxy lost` the moment this new test module's own independent `ros2_cli.generate()` call happened after crossing a pytest test-item boundary following the *existing* suite's own single canonical empy call site (`conftest.py`'s session-scoped `generated_dir` fixture) - `empy`'s `Interpreter` tracks "did I install a `sys.stdout` proxy" in a *class*, not per-call, attribute, which desyncs from reality once pytest's own capturing swaps `sys.stdout` for a new object between test items; fixed by resetting that stale class flag in `_expand()` itself whenever the live `sys.stdout` isn't already one of empy's own proxies (confirmed none of the `.em` templates ever rely on that proxy - they only use `@(expr)`/`@[for/if]` substitution, never a bare `print()` inside a `@{...}` code block). **New tests**: `tests/fixtures_own/Leaf.msg`+`Branch.msg` (an unqualified, same-package nested reference, mirroring `test_msgs/msg/Nested.msg`'s own real `BasicTypes basic_types_value` shape) and `tests/test_ros2_nested.py` - drives `ros2_cli.generate()` twice, once per `.msg`, exactly like the real CMake extension's own `foreach()`, then actually compiles and links both independently-generated outputs together and round-trips a real value through `to_tickle`/`encode`/`decode`/`from_tickle`; a second test confirms `Ros2Resolver` writes no duplicate `Leaf.h`/`.c`/adapter file for the nested reference. Verified: full `tools/typesupport` pytest suite (`pytest tests/ -q`) - 57/57 green (55 pre-existing + 2 new), confirmed stable across repeated runs and both forward and reverse file-collection order (ruling out the em fix being order-dependent luck); real CI (`Test all`, `Check all`) confirmed green on the push containing this fix - no regressions to `rmw_tickle`'s own suite, the standard-dispatch-chain check, or the conformance suite's own still-`Nested.msg`-excluded run - though that conformance suite doesn't yet build anything that would actually exercise the new `Ros2Resolver` path itself (see below). **Explicitly not done in this pass** (still open, matching `tools/typesupport/PLAN.md`'s own "Out of scope" list, unchanged by this milestone): arrays of strings and arrays of nested message types - both need a genuinely new "variable-size array element" field kind in `adapt.py`/`model.py`/`emit.py`, not just resolver plumbing; `test_msgs/msg/Nested.msg` has not yet been un-excluded from `.github/scripts/rmw-conformance-patches/test_msgs_rmw_tickle.patch`'s own generation list, so real CI hasn't exercised this milestone's own new capability through a real interface package yet, only through the local pytest suite above - `Arrays.msg`/`MultiNested.msg`/`BoundedSequences.msg`/`UnboundedSequences.msg` still need the array work above and must stay excluded; cross-package (`-I`) nested resolution beyond the two bundled builtins was, at the time this row was written, implemented in `Ros2Resolver`/`ros2_cli.py`'s own new `-I` flag but not yet wired into the real CMake extension - **superseded by Milestone 43, which did exactly that wiring and confirmed it with a real cross-package CI round-trip** (`rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c_tests_dep`/`Leaf.msg` nested via `Branch.msg`) - not a remaining gap of this row's own any more, this note is kept only as a record of what this milestone itself left open at the time. **Only remaining open item from this row, as of Milestone 54's own real-world confirmation it's still broken**: the array-of-string/array-of-nested-message-type grammar gap - `rcl_interfaces_rmw_tickle.patch` (Milestone 54) had to work around both with explicit `# @capacity <N>` annotations on eight real fields rather than this gap being closed. | 1, 15, 43 | 🔶 same-package single nested field verified locally + real CI green (no regressions); | **39** | 🔶 **Array-of-string support in `tools/typesupport`'s core generator** - Milestone 38's own "not yet done" list, item 1 (array-of-nested-type, `Nested.msg` un-exclusion, and `-I` CMake wiring remain open, tracked there). Design locked in by "TickLE Plan" (relayed to and approved by the user before any code was written, per this session's own standing cross-session-message discipline): no new wire format - DESIGN.md's existing "Variable/Fixed arrays" rule composes directly with its own "Strings" rule, one element per string (`align2 → uint16 count → pad2 → [string_1]...[string_N]`, each `string_i` encoded exactly like today's scalar string field) - explicitly rejecting both a wider uint32 count/length (no wire-size benefit, the same "fits in one datagram" reasoning DESIGN.md already uses for string length applies equally to array counts) and an offset/index table for random access (pure overhead at TickLE's one-datagram message scale, and real OMG CDR itself doesn't do this for string sequences either). **Implemented as `tools/typesupport/PLAN.md`'s own Milestone M7** (this generator's core grammar is tracked there, not here - see that file for the full design/verification writeup): new `WireField.array_element_kind` ("scalar" \| "string"); `model.py`/`layout.py`/`emit.py` gained the string-array-only branches this needs, guarded so an existing scalar array's own generated output is byte-for-byte unchanged (`test_golden.py` confirmed); a real clang-tidy cognitive-complexity finding on `*_encode`/`*_decode`/`*_encode_size` (once a struct has more than one or two string-array fields) fixed via `emit.emit_string_element_helpers()` - three small `static` per-struct helper functions generated directly into that struct's own `.c` file, never a new shared runtime dependency. A *bounded* string element (`string<=N[]`) is explicitly out of scope for this pass (not needed by any real target file - even after this milestone, `Arrays.msg`/`BoundedSequences.msg`/`UnboundedSequences.msg`/`MultiNested.msg` all still need array-of-nested-type support too, so they stay excluded from the conformance suite regardless). **The real ROS 2 pipeline still can't use this end to end**: `ros2_adapter.py`'s own converter generator explicitly rejects an array-of-string field now (`render_adapter()` raises `NotImplementedError`, tested) rather than silently generating wrong C - `rosidl_runtime_c` represents a string array completely differently from a primitive one (`rosidl_runtime_c__String__Sequence`/a plain `rosidl_runtime_c__String[N]`, neither a flat `T*` buffer nor a `rosidl_runtime_c__<T>__Sequence`), and converting between that shape and TickLE's own `char*[N]` isn't implemented here - a separate, adapter-layer follow-on, not a core-grammar one. "Supported subset" (above) updated to reflect this split (core-supported, adapter-unsupported) rather than leaving the stale "arrays of strings" blanket exclusion in place. Verified: full `tools/typesupport` pytest suite (`pytest tests/ -q`) - 63/63 green (57 pre-existing + 6 new: 2 roundtrip incl. an empty-string element, 1 default-value, 3 capacity-rejection incl. a NULL element and a malformed wire count, 1 confirming `ros2_adapter.render_adapter()`'s own explicit rejection), confirmed stable across repeated runs and both forward/reverse file-collection order; real clang-tidy/clang-format clean on the new generated golden fixtures (the cognitive-complexity finding above was caught and fixed this way, not assumed clean). **Real CI** (`Test all`, `Check all`) confirmed green on the push containing this milestone, after fixing one real, previously-latent gap it surfaced: `tests/golden/*.h` files have no `compile_commands.json` entry (never part of any real `make all` build), so `Check all`'s own repo-wide clang-tidy scan (`cpp-linter-action`) failed outright (`'tickle/config.h' file not found`) on the two new golden headers rather than just flag a style issue - reproduced locally against a *pre-existing* golden header (`Arrays.h`) with no compile database too, confirming this wasn't specific to the new files' content, just the first time any golden `.h` had been added/touched since this exact CI check started being able to catch it. Fixed with a new `tests/golden/compile_flags.txt` (clang-tidy auto-discovers this in a file's own directory before searching a parent for `compile_commands.json` - no CI workflow change needed) - verified locally against every existing golden header plus the two new ones, all clean. **Not yet done, still open** (unrelated to the CI-green confirmation above - no conformance-patch change was made or needed for this pass, since the real target files still need array-of-nested-type too, so none could be un-excluded yet): the `ros2_adapter.py` conversion gap above, and array-of-nested-type itself. | 38 | ✅ core generator (M7) verified locally + real CI green (no regressions); `ros2_adapter.py` conversion and array-of-nested-type still open |

| **41** | ✅ **`ros2_adapter.py`'s own array-of-string conversion** - "TickLE Plan"'s own priority list, item 2, closing the gap Milestone 39 (`tools/typesupport/PLAN.md`'s M7) deliberately left open: the core generator accepted an array-of-string field, but the real ROS 2 converter explicitly rejected one (`render_adapter()` raised `NotImplementedError`) since `rosidl_runtime_c` represents a string array completely differently from a primitive one. **Design**: a fixed ROS 2 string array (`string[N]`) is a plain in-place `struct rosidl_runtime_c__String name[N]` (no Sequence wrapper, matching TickLE's own no-count-prefix "Fixed arrays" wire rule); `_from_tickle_field_lines()` calls `rosidl_runtime_c__String__Sequence__init()` (variable only - a fixed array's `struct rosidl_runtime_c__String[N]` needs no separate allocation call) then `rosidl_runtime_c__String__assign()` per element, both mirroring a plain string field's own existing ownership-transfer contract. `needs_rosidl_string()`/`sequence_element_types()` updated so an array-of-string field pulls in exactly `string_functions.h` (already correct for a plain string field) and is explicitly excluded from the *primitive* Sequence header list (`rosidl_runtime_c__None__Sequence` would otherwise have silently leaked in as a bogus header name - guarded before it ever could). | **42** | ✅ **Array-of-nested-message-type support in `tools/typesupport`'s core generator** - "TickLE Plan"'s own priority list, item 3, the last core-grammar gap named there. **Implemented as `tools/typesupport/PLAN.md`'s own Milestone M8** (this generator's core grammar is tracked there, not here - see that file for the full design/verification writeup): composes the existing "Variable/Fixed arrays" rule with the existing "Nested messages" rule verbatim - each element delegates to that nested type's own already-generated `_encode`/`_decode`, so no new per-element size bookkeeping was needed even for a variable-size nested element. **Found and fixed two real bugs before/while implementing, not assumed away**: (1) a nested *array* element needs its type's own overall self-alignment, not just its first field's (DESIGN.md's own existing "Nested messages" rule was only ever correct for a *single* occurrence) - a real, latent distinction this codebase had never exercised before (every nested type used so far happened to have its own largest-aligned field first); (2) a deliberately oddly-shaped test fixture (`OddAlign.msg`) then exposed a second, more general, entirely pre-existing bug the first fix's own reasoning predicted: the generated `_Static_assert(sizeof(struct X) == wire_size)` is simply wrong whenever a fixed-size struct's own last field doesn't end on a multiple of the struct's own self-alignment (ordinary C compiler trailing padding `wire_size` itself correctly never counts) - fixed via a new `layout.padded_wire_size()`, used only in that one assert; confirmed via `test_golden.py` that this perturbs no existing generated output (every pre-existing fixed-size message's own wire_size already happened to be self-aligned). The same clang-tidy cognitive-complexity fix Milestone 39/M7 established (`emit.emit_string_element_helpers()`) is repeated here (`emit.emit_nested_array_element_helpers()`) for the identical reason, once a struct has more than one or two array-of-nested-type fields. **`rosidl_typesupport_tickle_c`'s own ROS 2 converter (`ros2_adapter.py`) did not convert this shape as of this row's own original writing** - `rosidl_runtime_c` represents it differently again (a plain `struct <Type>[N]` fixed, or a per-message `rosidl_runtime_c__<pkg>__msg__<Type>__Sequence` variable, neither memcpy-able nor a shared primitive Sequence type) - `render_adapter()` explicitly rejected it (`NotImplementedError`, tested), the exact same precedent set for array-of-string (Milestone 39→41's own arc) rather than silently generating wrong C. Verified: full `tools/typesupport` pytest suite green (67/67, stable across repeated runs and reverse file-collection order); a hand-derived expected encode_size (109 bytes, worked out field-by-field by hand before ever running the generator) independently confirmed by both a standalone C compile+run harness and the committed pytest roundtrip test; real clang-tidy/clang-format clean on the new generated golden fixtures. **Retroactive correction, found only while investigating Milestone 54's own priority-list follow-up (2026-09-20, real `git log -S` confirmed)**: `ros2_adapter.py`'s own array-of-nested-message-type conversion was in fact implemented the same day, commit `419e943` ("Implement ros2_adapter.py's array-of-nested-message-type conversion") - almost certainly as an unlabeled prerequisite of Milestone 50's own `/parameter_events` work (`ParameterEvent.msg`'s own `Parameter[] new_parameters` et al. are exactly this shape), but never recorded in `PLAN.md` under any milestone number at the time, leaving this row's own "still open" text stale - directly confirmed still-working today via Milestone 54's own real `rcl_interfaces` colcon build (`Parameter[] parameters`/`ParameterDescriptor[] descriptors` et al., all explicit-`@capacity`, all compiled+linked clean in real CI). **What is genuinely still open, as of that same investigation**: not a code gap at all any more (core generator, `ros2_adapter.py` conversion for both array shapes, and cross-package `-I` CMake wiring - Milestone 43 - are all real and confirmed working) - only `test_msgs`' own `Arrays.msg`/`MultiNested.msg`/`BoundedSequences.msg`/`UnboundedSequences.msg`/`Arrays.srv` remaining excluded from `test_msgs_rmw_tickle.patch`'s own generation list, which now needs only the same "add explicit `# @capacity <N>` annotations, since auto-derivation is a permanent by-design limit for these element kinds" treatment Milestone 54's own `rcl_interfaces_rmw_tickle.patch` just proved works, not any further generator work. | 38, 39, 41 | ✅ core generator (M8) verified locally via the full local pytest suite + a hand-derived, independently-confirmed encode_size;
